REVENUE OF THE DEPARTMENT

The major sources of revenue to this department are :

(i) Stamp duty payable on documents relating to various transctions entered in to by the
public.

(ii) Court fees payble on suits filed in the court.

(iii) Registration fees payble for registration of documents and fees for grant of encum-
brance certificates and certified copies, ect.

(iv) Charges for collection of transfer duty (surcharge)

Explanation :- For the purpose of the clause (a) of this article, notwithstanding anything
contained in any judgement, decree or order of any court or order of any authourity, any
letter, note, memorandum or writing relating to the deposit of title deeds whether written
or made either before or at the time when or after the deposit of title deeds is effected and
whether it is in respect of the security for the first loan or any additional loan or loans taken

shall be deemed to be the amount of total value of the shares issued or allotted
by the transferee company, either in exchange or otherwise, and the amount of
consideration, if any, paid for such amalgamation or merger.

the document by the seller and builder or market value, whichever is higher.

GOVERNMENT OF ANDHRA PRADESH
ABSTRACT

Revenue (R&S) Department - Registration Act, 1908 - Section 78 - Table of Fees - Revision
- Simplification and Rationalization of fee structure - Orders - Issued.
REVENUE (REGN-I) DEPARTMENT
G.0.Ms.No.463 Dated: 17.08.2013
Read the following:

 

1) G.0.Ms.No.757, Revenue (Regn.|) Deptt. dt. 18.12.2001.

2) G.O.Ms.No.476, Revenue (Regn.I) Deptt, dt. 25.07.2012.

3) G.O.Ms.No.1188, Revenue (Regn.|) Deptt., dt. 08.08.2012.
ORDER:

The following notification will be published in an Extra-ordinary issue of the Andhra
Pradesh Gazette Dated: 26.08.2013.

NOTIFICATION

In exercise of the powers conferred by section 78 of the Registration Act, 1908 (Act
XVI of 1908), and in supersession of the orders issued in G.O.Ms.No. 757, Revenue (Regn-I)
Department, dated: 18.12.2001 and as subsequently amended from time to time and also
the order issued in G.0.Ms.No. 476, Revenue (Regn-!) Department, dated 25.07.2012, the
Governor of Andhra Pradesh hereby notifies the following rates of fees as shown in the "
Table of Fees".

The notification hereby made shall come into force with effect on and from
01.09.2013.

 

REGISTRATION FEES
The prescribed rates of Registration Fees are as follows :

duly paying required fee prescribed.

NOTE :

1. Allother documents, not susceptible to money valuation, attracts a fee of Rs.1,000/-

2. All other documents, susceptible to valuation and not covered in categories (1) to (3)
of Table of Fees, is leviable with a fee of Rs.500/-

3. As defined under Section 56(2) of I.T. Act, 1961, the following persons are relatives
and gifts executed in their favour are chargeable to duty at 2% on M.V. :

(i) Spouse of the individual;

(ii) Brother or sister of the individual;

(iii) Brother or sister of the spouse of the individual;

(iv) Brother or sister of either of the parents of the individual;

(v) Any lineal ascendant or descendant of the individual;

(vi) Any lineal ascendant or descendant of the spouse of the individual;
(vii) Spouse of the person referred to in clauses (ii) to (vi).

4. Transfer duty is also leviable @ 1.5% besides stampduty (except contonment area,
where T.D is 5%) for the sale deeds, gift deeds, lease in perpetuity, mortgage with
possession and exchange deeds.

TRANSFER DUTY

 

1. The rate of duty on Transfer of Property in respect of Panchayats in the state has
been reduced from 3% to 1.5% w.e.f. 01.04.2013 vide G.O.Ms No.226 Panchayat Raj
& Rural Development (PTS.|) dept. dt.06.04.2013

2. The rate of duty on Transfer of Property in respect of Gift deeds in favour of Relatives
as defined U/s 56 of Income Tax and Govt, local bodies and UDAs in respect of
panchayats in the state has been reduced from 1.5% to 0.5% w.e.f. 01.04.2013 vide
G.0.Ms No.463Panchayat Raj & Rural Development (PTS.I) dept. dt.19.12.2013 with
immediate effect.

3. The rate of duty on Transfer of Property in respect of all the Municipal corporation
areas in the state has been fixed at 1.5% without deduction towards collection
charges w.e.f. 01.04.2013 vide G.0.Ms No.150Municipal Administration and Urban
Development (TC) Dept dt.06.04.2013.

4. The rate of duty on Transfer of Property in respect of all Selection /Special Grade
Municipalities in the state has been fixed at 1.5% without deduction towards
collection charges w.e.f. 01.04.2013 vide G.O.Ms No.151 Municipal Administration
and Urban Development (TC) Dept dt.06.04.2013.

5. The rate of duty on Transfer of Property in respect of all the Municipalities (other
than Selection /Special Grade Municipalities) in the state has been fixed at 1.5%
without deduction towards collection charges w.e.f. 01.04.2013 vide G.O.Ms No.152
Municipal Administration and Urban Development (TC) Dept dt.06.04.2013.

6. The rate of duty on Transfer of Property payable on all sale deeds of flats/ Apartments
in Municipal areas in the state has been fixed at 1.5% without deduction towards
collection charges w.e.f. 01.04.2013 vide G.O.Ms No.153 Municipal Administration
and Urban Development (TC) Dept dt.06.04.2013.

PROCEEDINGS OF THE COMMISSIONER & INSPECTOR GENERAL OF
REGISTRATION AND STAMPS, ANDHRA PRADESH, VIJAYAWADA

Present: Sri V.Ramakrishna, 1.R.S.,

 

Proceedings No.MV1/752/2022 Dated: 31-5-2023.

Sub:- MARKET VALUE SCHEME- Registration and Stamps Department- Revision of
Structure Rates for various types of buildings for assessing Market Value-
Communication for implementation- Orders-Issued.

Ref: 1. G.0.Ms. No.301, Revenue (Regn.|) Department, Dt. 04.05.1998.

2. Memo.No. REVO1-REGSOMISC/106/2023-REGN-1, dt.31-05-2023 from
Revenue (REGN.1) Dept., A.P.

3. Minutes of the structural rates revision committee.

kK
ORDERS:-

1) Through the reference 2nd cited, the Government had accorded permission to revise
the market value of certain areas in the state under special revision(Rule 9) where
huge gap exists between open market values with that of departmental guidelines
values and also to revise the structure values in the state w.e.f.01.06.2023 and rule
2(4)(d) a committee has met on 31.05.2023 and recommended revised structure
rates.

2) Accordingly, after careful examination of the recommendations of the committee
vide reference 3 cited, it is decided to implement the revised construction rates
of the structures and buildings as detailed in Annexure under the A.P. Revision of
Market Value Guidelines Rules, 1998. The make effect date for the new rates will
from 01.06.2023.

3) The Registering Officers and Authorized Officers for post facto spot inspection
of properties shall also follow the rates shown in the Annexure for assessing
constructions cost of structures and buildings.

4) The receipt of the orders shall be acknowledged forthwith.
Encl.:- As above.

Sd/- V.Ramakrishna
Commissioner & Inspector General of
Registration and Stamps, A.P., Vijayawada

Note:-1. For availing the depreciation for structures under non industrial use, a certificate
regarding the age proof should be obtained from the competent authorities l.e.,
Panchayat Offices, Municipal Corporations etc. and filed in the separate file in serial
orders, and particulars of certificate in respect of age proof should also be incorporate
in the document.

2. For availing the depreciation for structures under Industrial use, a certificate
regarding the year of construction should be obtained from the competent authorities
l.e., Panchayat Offices, Municipal Corporations etc. and filed in the separate file in
serial orders, and particulars of certificate in respect of age proof should also be
incorporate in the document.

 

 

 
 

Se

Provided that in respect of the cases mentioned in Note 1 and 2 above, where age of
the structure is supported by any link document or Occupancy Certificate issued by
competent authority, verification of a separate certificate is not necessary.

 

3. For fixation of Composite value to apartments, the rate of Rs 1400,1200 and 850 per
square feet fixed to RCC structures of GF and FF for different areas shall be taken
as base value and the revised composite rate shall be got fixed by the respective
Committees by applying the existing formula of 1/20th revised site rate + Rs.1400/-
sqft (base value of structure rate). For the structures 3rd floor onwards, Rs 30, Rs 25
and Rs 20 shall be applicable for each floor. |.e., composite value + 30 for 3rd floor,
composite value + 60 for 4th floor and composite value + 90 for 5th floor so on for the
structures in Col No 2. The same analogy shall be followed for Col No 3 and 4 also.

4. The composite value shall not be applicable for the door numbers under commercial
(02) classification included in Form-ll.

Memo No: MV1/752/2022 Dt: 06.06.2023,

Sub:-Registration and Stamps Department Market Value Scheme Revision of Structure
rates for various types of buildings for assessing market value - Further
instructions - Reg.

Ref: 1. C&IG R&S Circular Memo No: MV1/138/2012, dt: 26.7.2012.
2. C&IG R&S Circular Memo No MV1/4497/2015 dated 08.7.2015
3. C&IG R&S, Proceedings No: MV1/752/2022, dt: 31.5.2023.

OK

Through the reference 1"cited, instructions were issued regarding procedure for
calculation of structure value by applying Composite rate. In the said instructions,
inter-alia it was instructed to fix the composite value for flats, taking the construction
rate for that area prescribed plus 1/20th of the Sq. Yard rate proposed for that locality.

 
Se

2. Through the reference 2nd cited, instructions were issued regarding fixation of
higher composite rate for the ground and 1st floors situated in commercial zones as
declared by the local bodies.

 

3. Now through the reference 3rd cited, revised construction rates of the structures and
buildings as recommended by the Committee under Rule 4(2)(d) of A.P. Revision of
M.V. Guideline Rules, 1998 were communicated for implementation w.e.f: 1.6.2023.
Among other guidelines, it was instructed that the composite value shall not be
applicable for the door numbers under commercial (02) classification included in
Form-ll.

4. In partial modification of the note 4 of the ref 3 cited, the following instructions are
Issued with regard to fixation of composite values for commercial (02) classification
included in Form-ll.

“Composite value shall be fixed as per the existing procedure while duly following
the instructions at Sl.No.17 issued vide ref 2nd cited, for the door numbers under
commercial (02) classification included in Form-11."

5. All the Deputy Inspectors General of R&S, District Registrars are instructed to guide
the Sub Registrars under their control in the above matter.

V Rama Krishna
Commissioner and Inspector General of
Registration and Stamps, A.P., Vijayawada.

Office Of The Commissioner And Inspector General
Of Registration And Stamps, A.P., Vijayawada.

CIRCULAR No: CARD -HM/SM- 1/2023, Dated: 31/12/2022

Sub : - Registration and Stamps Dept. - CARD Project — Software Applications for
registration of marriages under Hindu Marriage Act, in 1955 and the Special
Marriage Act, 1954— Launched on 01-01-2023 — Registration of marriages
only through software modules — Instructions issued — Reg.

OK

Registration and Stamps Department registers marriages under the Hindu Marriage
Act, 1955 and the Special Marriage Act, 1954. Until now the process of registering
marriages was done manually. Now a software module have been developed for
registration of marriages under the Acts and was tested by Marriage Registrars in the
state and having satisfied with the performance of the applications the two modules are
being launched on 02-01-2023 in the portal of the department (registration.ap.gov.in).
From 02.01.2023 onwards the registration of marriages shall be processed only through
the software modules by the marriage registrars in the state. The Registered number
of marriage starts from 1 of 2023 which is automatically generated by the module. No
manual application shall be entertained. The application forms will be filled by the parties
online through the respective modules and upload all the required documents through the
modules. The parties also book the slot and visit the office . In case any walk in marriage
application, the registrar shall help/guide the parties by filing the required application
form and scan the supporting documents in the online module provided till the system
stabilizes.

The Hindu Marriage application will be generated by the module and the marriage
registrar has to take a printout of the same, obtain signatures of the parties and witnesses
on the application and shall upload the scanned image of the same through the module.
A marriage certificate will be generated and the marriage registrar shall print it, affixes
signature and seal and issues to the parties. No manual marriage register need be
maintained. Extracts of marriage register and copies of supporting documents can be
retrieved from the database and certified copies of the same can be issued to the parties
as and when applied for.

Under the Special Marriage Act, 1954, the notices under Section 8 under Section
16 shall be filled by the parties online and will be submitted to the marriage registrar
concerned. The marriage registrar shall take required printouts from the module and
takes further action as per the procedure. Sending notices to the authorities and receiving
intimation regarding publication of the notices from them is also automated. The marriage
declaration and the marriage certificate are automatically generated. Copies of documents
can be retrieved from the database and certified copies can be issued to the parties as
and when applied for.

 
&  °» .»”»”»+x,x}LFEZ 7 Ts

The payment of the requisite fee under both the Acts will be done through CFMS
portal online with the help of CFMS payment gateway As detailed in the online application.
The fee can also be paid at the Sub Registrar office in case of any difficulty on payment
through CFMS portal.

The User manuals are transmitted herewith. All the District Registrars, Sub-Registrars
in the state are directed to widely publicize the user manual and procedure for the online
application and slot booking. The receipt of Circular must be acknowledged forthwith.

 

V Rama Krishna
Commissioner and Inspector General of
Registration and Stamps, A.P., Vilayawada.

 

swotin moctn 24S Dardr Sepos? snty,eo — Sad} Haiveaso.

Office Of The Commissioner And Inspector General
Of Registration And Stamps, A.P., Vijayawada.

Memo.No.G2/1949439/2022 Dated:08/01/2023

Sub: Registration and Stamps Department - Revision of the Hindu Marriage Act and
the Special Marriage Act - Committee constituted - Reg.

Ref :

OK

It has been proposed to review and revise the Hindu Marriage Act and the Special
Marriage Act in the light of making the registration of marriages online,

Accordingly the following committee is here by constituted to study the existing
provisions under the Acts and to suggest suitable changes in Act and rules which are
redundant and to make the registration procedure online.

1. DIG.Prakasam.
2. DR. VZM.

3. DR Rajahmundry
4. DR. NLR

5. DR. CTR.

6. DR. MTM

The committee shall submit recommendations in 2 weeks time

V Rama Krishna
Commissioner and Inspector General of
Registration and Stamps, A.P., Vijayawada.

Office The Commissioner And Inspector General
Of Registration And Stamps, A.P., Vijayawada.

Memo.No.G2/1951078/2022 Dated:11/01/2023.

Sub :- Registration and Stamps Department Relocation of SRO offices which are
unsuitable to house the Government offices Committee Constituted to identify
suitable premises and send proposals - Reg.

OK
Most of the Sub-Registrar offices in the state were established in the existing
premises some 30-50 years back in places which were in the outskirts then. With the
expansion of cities and towns in course of time, they have now come to be located in the
center of activity, where public and commercial activity is rampant. This is causing a lot of
inconvenience to the registration public and the staff in equal measure, and the problem
is compounded by vehicular traffic and public activity, etc.

Many requests have been received from the staff of the department and the general
public to shift such Sub Registrar Offices which are located in dungeon locations with
narrow lanes and by lanes with almost no room for parking.

It has therefore been decided to constitute a committee with the following officers
to identify such offices in each district which are required to identify new premises for
relocation of such offices and shift to new premises immediately.

1. District Registrars,
2. District Registrars (MV & Audit),
3. Sub Registrars Concerned.

The above committee is directed to identify the Sub Registrar Offices which are up
for shifting as per above parameters and identify suitable premises with enough office
space, reasonable outer space with compound, parking space, ventilation etc., and send
proposals to the undersigned, for further examination of the matter before 31.01.2023
without fail.

V Rama Krishna
Commissioner and Inspector General of
Registration and Stamps, A.P., Vijayawada.

Memo. No. G1/1727110/2022 Dt.25/01/2023

Sub:- Registration and Stamps Department - NAVARATHNALU - Delivery of
Registration services in Village Secretariats - Refreshment Training to
Panchayat Secretaries and Digital Assistants for 51 villages under Phase-1
and 1949 villages under Phase-2-Instructions - Issued - Reg.

OK

Kind attention of all the D.I.Gs and District Registrars in the state is invited to the
subject cited.

They are informed that as a part of delivering registration services in Village
Secretariats under NAVARATNALU, it is decided to provide Comprehensive Refreshment
training to Panchayat Secretaries and Digital Assistants in 51 villages under Phase-1 and
1949 villages under Phase-2 in the respective Sub registrar Offices/District Registrar
Offices from 30-01-2023. The participants must be trained in the delivery of registration
services at VS&WS respectively.

Therefore, all DISG & D.RS are here by instructed to impart practical training to list
of participants at respective SROS/DROs from 30.01.2023 without fail. The list of Villages
and the District Co-ordinators from VSWS is enclosed herewith.

V Rama Krishna
Commissioner and Inspector General of
Registration and Stamps, A.P., Vijayawada.

The Government is implementing the biometric attendance linked salaries to the
Village/ Ward Secretariat Functionaries and accordingly certain instructions are issued
from time to time vide references cited.

All the Drawing and Disbursing Officers (DDOs) of the Village/ Ward Secretariats are
requested to adhere to the following instructions on payment of salaries linked with Bio
Metric attendance for the period from 01-02-2023 to 28-02-2023 to be paid on 1 March
2023.

1. The Bio Metric attendance period cycle for the January 2023 is from 23-01- 2023 to
22-02-2023 (the attendance period cycle mentioned is only for purpose of determining
the attendance of the employee. Based on attendance days in above cycle, the salary
needs to be paid proportionately for the month of February 2023 from 01-02-2023 to
28-02-2023)

 
 

&  °» ».»”»”»+x%xLEE

2. Deduct the salary for the un authorized absent days in the attendance cycle period
i.e. from 23-01-2023 to 22-02-2023. (Absent days Total days in attendance calendar
(present days holidays leaves + attendance authorized & Regularized))

 

3. HRMS application and logins were provided to all the Village /Ward Secretariat
functionaries to apply for CL, optional Holiday, Medical & Maternity Leave and for
regularizing the attendance in case of Trainings, Bio Metric failures, Meetings, On
duty etc.

4. Attendance dashboard is provided in the GSWS website (https://
gramawardsachivalayam.ap.gov.in/GSWSDASHBOARD/#!/Attendance
ReportMandal) for checking the day wise attendance.

5. All the DDOS are requested to pay the salaries to all the Village / Ward Secretariat
functionaries (including old Panchayat secretaries, old VROS, old Municipal
Employees) based on the attendance reports available in the above report (Mentioned
in point No4).

6. Consider the leaves and attendance authorized shown in the above dashboard
attendance report as "Present"

7. Causal Leave, Optional holidays, Medical leave and Maternity leave are enabled in
HRMS application. Calculate other leaves manually and consider them as "Present"
while paying the salary.

All the Drawing and Disbursing Officers (DDOS) of the Village/ Ward Secretariat
functionaries are instructed to ensure the above guidelines accordingly while uploading
and submitting the salary bills to the Treasury. Any deviations from the above guidelines
shall be viewed seriously.

All the District Collectors as well as District Treasury Officers are requested to
implement above guidelines while paying the salaries to the Village/ Ward secretariat
functionaries for the month of February 2023 to paid on 1 March 2023.

GOVERNMENT OF ANDHRA PRADESH
REVENUE (LANDS.I) DEPARTMENT

Memo No. REVO1-LANAOLAND(PM)/8/2020-LANDS-| Dated : 23/03/2022

Sub: Lands-Certain instructions/guidelines regarding ‘Dotted lands and deletion of
lands from the list of prohibited Properties notified U/s 22-A (1) of Registration)
Act, 1908-Further instructions issued -Reports called for - Regarding.

Ref: 1. Government orders notifying the list of properties recommended under
Section 22-A(1)(e) of Registration Act, 1908 of all Districts.

2. A.P.Dotted lands (Updation in Re-settlement Register) Act, 2017 (Act No.
10 of 2017)

G.0.Ms.No. 298, Revenue (Assgn.1) Dept., Dt.17.07.2017.

4. Govt.Circular Memo No.57443/Assn.1/2012, Revenue (Assignment-1)
Department, dated.06.11.2018.

5. Govt. Circular Memo No.57443/Assn.1/2012, Revenue (Assignment-1)
Department, dated.04.01.2019.

6. Govt.Memo.No.REVO1-LANAOLAND(NOTF)/5/2019-ASSN.I, Rev (Assn.)
Dept., dt.18.11.2019.

7. Memo.No.REVO1/LANA/19/2020-SECY-LANDS-Endt. dt.21-12-2020.
8. Memo No. REVO1-LANAOLAND(PM)/8/2020-LANDS-|.Dated:18-02-2021

OK

1. The attention of the Spl. CS & Chief Commissioner of Land Administration, A.P. and
the District Collectors is invited to the subject and references cited. They are informed
that several representations were received by the Government from members of the
public that lands owned by them for long periods of time and with documentary proof
of the same, were erroneously notified under clause 22A(1) (a) to (e), resulting in
registration authorities refusing to register any documents pertaining to the notified
survey numbers. Government continues to receive multiple representations on a
continuous basis on the same subject.

2. In order to rectify the situation, the Government enacted "The A.P. Dotted Lands
(Updation in Re-settlement Register) Act, 2017 (Dotted Lands Act, for short)" and
the same has come into force with effect from 14th June, 2017 (vide G.0.Ms.No.210,

————

 
 

Revenue (Assignment.1) Dept dt14-06-2017). In follow up, Government have issued
“The A.P. Dotted Lands (Updation in Re-settlement Register) Rules 2017 vide G.0.Ms.
No.298 Revenue (Assn.1) Department, dt.17.07.2017.

In the references cited 4th to 8th above, certain instructions/clarifications were
issued from time to time to guide disposal of claims filed under the provisions of A.P.
Dotted lands ( Updation in Re-settlement Register) Act, 2017.

This circular memo is issued in supersession of all previously issued memos and
circulars on this subject, including those cited in the references 4th to 8th above.

Rule 7 of the Dotted Lands Act reads as follows:
7. Deletion U/s 22-A (1) of the Registration Act, 1908:

(a) The District collector shall furnish the list of lands to the Registering officer
concerned for deletion from the prohibitory lists already furnished to the
Registration Department u/s 22-A(1) of the Registration Act, 1908 within
one month of the date of the order under section 7 (or) 8 of the Act as the
case may be.

(b) The Registering officer concerned shall delete the lands as furnished by the
District collector under rule 7(a) from the prohibitory lists furnished under
section 22-A(1) ofthe Registration Act, 1908.

After careful examination, Government hereby clarifies that cases for deletion from
prohibitory lists, under any of the sections 22-A(1)(a) to (e), shall be dealt with and
disposed by Collectors in terms of the A.P. Dotted Lands (Updation in Re-settlement
Register) Act, 2017 without further reference to the CCLA or the Government. All
pending cases at the level of the CCLA and Government shall be returned back to
Collectors for disposal accordingly.

Section 4(3) of the Dotted lands act reads as below:

4. (3) The entry in Column No.(16) of Re-Settlement Register in respect of Dotted
Lands which are not covered under sub-section (1) and (2) and are in possession
of any person shall be replaced and updated by making an entry in the name of the
person, in terms of the orders issued under section 7 or section 8 as the case may
be;

Explanation:- Continuous possession for a period of twelve years or more prior to
the commencement of this Act by a person (present claimant or his predecessor in
interest), based on documents shall be sufficient for updation of his name in column
No.(16) of Re-Settlement Register in respect of Dotted Lands covered under sub-
section (3).

 

 

 
i !

Documents as mentioned in the Explanation in Section 4(3) of the Dotted Lands Act
are defined in Section 2(a) of the Dotted Lands Act:

2. Definitions - In this Act, unless the context otherwise requires, -

(a) “Documents” means registered documents, entries in Register of holdings
maintained by the Registration department, 10(1) account and Record of Rights
maintained by the Revenue Department and order of decree of any court of
competent authority.

Government hereby clarifies once again that any one of the Documents mentioned
in Section 2(a) can constitute conclusive proof of continuous possession for a period
of twelve years or more prior to the commencement of this Act. Multiple documents
shall not be insisted upon.

7. Section 6 of the AP Record of rights in Land and Pattadar Passbooks Act (ROR Act, for
short) reads as under:

a. Presumption of correctness of entries in record of rights. - Every entry in record
of rights shall be presumed to be true until the contrary is proved or until it is
otherwise amended in accordance with the provisions of this Act.....

Government hereby clarifies that the Record of Rights maintained by the Revenue
Department, alone can constitute conclusive proof of continuous possession for a
period of twelve years or more prior to the commencement of this Act, if the Record
of Rights entry can be traced back for 12 years before the critical date mentioned in
the Dotted Lands Act 2017.

Government also clarifies that in terms of Section 6 of the AP Record of rights in Land
and Pattadar Passbooks Act, Collectors shall presume the correctness of the existing
Record of rights maintained by the Revenue department until the contrary is proved
or until it is amended in accordance with the provisions of the RoR Act.

8. The District Collectors are directed to expeditiously dispose all pending cases and
cases that come up before them in future. The Spl. CS & Chief Commissioner of
Land Administration is directed to review this item of work on a weekly basis with
Collectors to ensure expeditious disposal of matters.

G Sai Prasad IAS
Chief Commissioner

CIRCULAR Memo No:CARD/e-1730463 Dt.#ApprovedDate#

Sub: Registration & Stamps Department-Registration of properties- | compliance
of Section 22B of Registration Act 1908, Integration of Records of Municipal/
Local Bodies and Registration data - Certain modifications on registration
of documents - verifying the House assessment No./VLT No./LPNo./IPLP
No. in the Municipal/ Local Bodies/ DTCP data base or Link document is
compulsory- Registrations based on LPM Numbers -Certain Instructions to
follow by Registering Officers —Issued- Regarding.

Ref: 1. This office Circular Memo No.G1/1249/2012, Dated 25-06-2016
1. This Office Circular Memo No.G1/9732/2013, Dated 07-09-2015.
2. Minutes of Meeting on Dated.06/05/2022 with MA&UD Department.

 
 

3. Act 15 of 2022(An Act to further amend Andhra Pradesh [Rights _ in land]
and Pattadar Pass Book Act,1971

4. CCLA Minutes dated. 07/03/2023.

5. CCLA Minutes dated. 15/03/2023

OK

The attention of the DIGs, DRs and Registering Officers in the State is invited to the
subject and references cited. Through the amendment of Section 22B of Registration Act
1908 pertaining to Andhra Pradesh certain duties are cast upon the Registering Officer
to prevent double Registrations. Through reference 1st cited certain instructions were
issued regarding, procedure to be taken while registering agriculture properties as per
Section 6D of Andhra Pradesh [Rights in land] and Pattadar Pass Book Act,1971 and
through reference 2nd _ cited on house properties of local bodies which had paved the
way to mutate the property accurately in the name of transferee and also prevented
the conveying of the same property by the same executant which is already conveyed. In
continuation to above instructions, the following further instructions are issued to ensure
that no double registrations takes place in contravention of Section 22B of Registration
Act,1908 i.e not to convey the same property by the same executant twice and securing
the title of the property. Further guidelines on the registration of properties based on LPM
Numbers also issued.

A. Procedure to be adopted in case of Agricultural properties:

1. For all the documents listed in Annexure | affecting agriculture properties , the
registering officer shall not entertain registrations if the name of the executant is
not reflected in the pattadar column of the webland electronic record maintained
by the Revenue Department. For certain documents where the above condition is
not fulfilled the procedure indicated in Annexure Il need to be adopted .

2. Simultaneously, the Registering officer shall also not accept registration of
any document if the property comprised therein has already been conveyed or
permanently alienated by the same person or his representative, assignee or
agent in favor of any other person by a registered document unless previous
registered document was cancelled by order of a court. In addition the Registering
Officer shall also take a declaration prescribed in Annexure III.

3. In the areas where resurvey is completed or Bhu Hakku Patras were issued,
Registrations shall be effected based on LPM Numbers only. No registration shall
be affected for part extent of an LPM and in all such cases sub-division shall
be carried out mandatorily before effecting registration. Further the Registering
Officer shall issue an endorsement in such cases requesting the parties to
submit documents for registration only after getting sub-division carried out by
Tahsildar.

 

 

 
 

 

4. The meta data of the documents registered based on the LPM numbers shall be
pushed automatically to the webland server to effect the auto mutation of the
records.

5. If the schedule of the property in the registration document pertains to more than
one LPM, the executant(s) and claimant(s) details shall be listed separately for
each LPM in the document as well as in meta data.

6. Executants/ Claimants (and if they are represented, the representative) Adhar
Numbers shall be captured at the time of Registration. For this purpose, e-KYC
shall be done for both Executant and Claimant and their representatives.

Procedure to be adopted in case of Non-Agricultural properties in urban
local bodies

Through reference 2nd cited certain instructions were issued to match the executants
particulars with the assessment number in order to register the Houses/Flats/
Buildings. These instructions were issued in order to comply with the S.0. 708 of the
A.P. Registrations manual Part-ll under Registration Act, 1908.

Through reference 3rd cited, it was decided to streamline the Urban property
registrations in terms of Assessment number (Property tax Identification Number) of
Houses/Flats/Buildings for any construction, Vacant land Tax Identification Number
for Urban vacant sites within Ward and Blocks, Lay-out Plan number, In Principle
Lay-out Pattern number in cases of House sites or plots for all other Non-Agricultural
properties in urban local bodies through integration with Municipal Administration
Department data.

The following further instructions are issued for all the documents affecting non-
agriculture properties in urban local bodies listed in Annexure I.

Registering officer shall not allow registrations in respect of Houses/Flats/ buildings
within urban local bodies unless the executants name finds place in the owners
column in the electronic record maintained by the urban local bodies. However for
newly constructed Houses/Flats/Buildings where the Assessment numbers have
not been allotted or for first time registration of such Houses/Flats/Buildings, the
building plan approval number has to be insisted in the document along with details
of names of the persons who got the permissions from urban local bodies to verify
the permission particulars with the details in the document. If such particulars in the
document match with permission particulars then only the Registering Officer shall
allow registrations.

In case the above condition is not fulfilled or Assessment Numbers/Building
permission numbers are not available for the Houses/Flats/Buildings in the electronic
record maintained by the urban local bodies then the Registering Officer shall cross
check whether Houses/Flats/Buildings property is registered with the help of Link
document provided by the public in the document.

 
& ° ».»”»+x=4}LEEZ 7

3. Only when above conditions i.e B.1 and B.2 are satisfied then only Registering Officer
shall allow registrations for Houses/Flats/ Buildings in urban local bodies.

 

4. When the Houses/Flats/Buildings data is not reflected in the electronic data
maintained by the urban local bodies, the Registering Officer shall advise the
registering public to get the electronic record updated in the urban local bodies
concerned data base for effecting registration.

5. In respect of vacant sites in urban local bodies ,the Registering Officer shall not
register documents unless the executant name or developers or land owners name
is reflected in the electronic record of vacant land Tax number or Layout permission
Number or In Principle Layout Pattern number of the urban local bodies concerned.

6. In cases of vacant sites where Assessment is not completed or Where Vacant Land
Tax number is not available, approved Layout plan details or In principle Layout
pattern details are to be incorporated in the document along with the particulars of
developers or land owners details and these are to be verified with the electronic
data of the concerned urban local bodies concerned .

7. Incases where layout permission numbers or In principle layout permission numbers
are not available for the vacant sites in urban local bodies, the link document details
need to be verified and if the executant and property details of the document match
with the details of the claimant in link document and property in the link document,
then only the Registering Officer shall proceed for Registration.

8. In any other case of non-agriculture property documents registrations, where the
executant name is not reflected in the electronic data maintained by the urban local
bodies the following procedure as indicated in Annexure IV to be adopted.

9. In case of non-agriculture property registrations of Houses/Flats/Buildings, vacant
sites/house site/plots in Gramakantams or plots in villages or in rural local bodies,
there is no need for the Registering Officer to insist upon Property Tax Identification
Number(PTIN),Vacant Land Tax Number({VLT No)/Layout Permission Number({LP
No.)/In Principle Layout Permission Number (IPLP No.) until the electronic data is
integrated.

All the Sub Registrars, District Registrars and Deputy Inspector Generals of
Registration & Stamps Department shall follow the instructions strictly and any deviations
will be viewed seriously.

#ApprovedByName#

Commissioner and Inspector General
Registration and Stamps, A.P., Vijayawada

CCLA's Ref.No.L.R.-II/ROR-II/144/2021, dt. 09/04/2022

Sub: ROR- errors in ROR- to correct only clerical errors as provide in The AP Rights in land
and Pattadar Passbooks Act 1971- Instructions issued-Reg

 

OK
The attention of the Collectors is invited to Sec 5(6) of The AP Rights in Land and

Pattadar Passbooks Act 1971. Keeping in view the provisions of the act the following
instructions are issued:

CORRECTION OF CLERICAL ERRORS

The purpose of this service is to correct clerical errors that have crept into the
electronic revenue records and hence the service shall be named "Correction of clerical
errors" instead of "Rectification of Entries in Record of Rights". As per Section 5 of the AP
Rights in Land & Pattadar Passbooks Act, 1971 the Tahsildar is competent to correct the
clerical errors in the Pattadar Passbooks.

Only the following clerical errors can be corrected using this service.

Any other changes to the electronic land records shall be considered under the
mutation service alone.

1. CASES WHERE AADHAR NUMBER IS NOT SEEDED: No change in name of
patatdar/pattadar's father's name/Pattadar's spouse's name/Khatadar/
khatadar's father's name/Khatadar's spouse's name/enjoyer/enjoyer's father's
name/enjoyer's spouse's name shall be affected unless their aadhar number has
already been seeded.

2. CASES WHERE AADHAR NUMBER IS SEEDED: In cases where aadhar number has
been seeded and a change of name is requested, the English & Telugu Names in
Aadhar database shall be adopted into the record, after aadhar based biometric
authentication.

3. AADHAR SEEDING SERVICE: A separate service to biometrically autheticate and
seed aadhar number shall be provided.

4. No correction shall be made to the electronic land records without biometric
authentication and electronic signature of the approving authority.

  
ly

5. The practice of handing over digital signature is absolutely disallowed and any
such action will attract disciplinary action.

6. Any change other in the existing record shall be carried out through the Mutation
procedure only.

All the Collectors in the state are requested to diligently follow the above instructions
and ensure the sanctity of digital records is maintained and the provisions of The AP
Rights in land and Pattadar Passbooks Act 1971 is strictly followed.

G Sai Prasad IAS
CCLA & Spl CS

THE ANDHRA PRADESH GAZETTE
NOTIFICATIONS BY GOVERNMENT

REVENUE DEPARTMENT
(LANDS-1)

CERTAIN AMENDMENTS PROPOSED TO THE ANDHRA PRADESH RECORD OF RIGHTS
(CERTIFICATE OF OWNERSHIP FOR GRAMAKANTAM LAND)
RULES, 2022 - PRELIMINARY NOTIFICATION.

[G.0.Ms.No.172, Revenue (Lands-1), 12th April, 2023.]
PRELIMINARY NOTIFICATION

In exercise of the powers conferred by the subsection (1) of section 11 of Andhra
Pradesh Rights in Land and Pattadar Pass Books Act, 1971 (Act No.26 of 1971), the
Government hereby proposed to amend the Andhra Pradesh Record of Rights. (Certificate
of Ownership for Gramakanlam Land) Rules, 2022 and here by published for the general
information in the Andhra Pradesh Gazette as required under sub- section (1) of Section
11 of the said Act.

Notice is hereby given that the aforesaid amendments will be taken into consideration
by the Government after the expiry of (12) days from the date of publication of this
notification in the Andhra Pradesh Gazette and that any objections or suggestions if any
received before the expiry of the aforesaid period will be considered by the Government
of the Andhra Pradesh.

————

 
 

 

& °°. ».».”»”»«+x=xFEZ 7

The Objections and suggestions should be addressed to the Special Chief Secretary
to Government. Revenue (Lands) Department. AP. Secretariat, Government of Andhra
Pradesh, Velagapudi

 

AMENDMENTS
In the said rules.-
1. Inrule 2, in sub-rule (1), clause (5) shall be omitted.

2. In rule 5, in sub-rule (1), in clauses (i), (ii). (iii) and (iv), for the expression
“Dy Tahsildar (Resurvey)", the expression "Tahsildar” shall be substituted.

3. In rule 11, in sub-rule (1), in clause (a), for the expression “Dy Tahsildar
(Resurvey)", the expression "Tahsildar" shall be substituted.

4. Inrule 15, in sub-rule (1), for the expression "Dy.Tahsildar (Resurvey)", the
expression "Tahsildar" shall be substituted

G. SAI PRASAD,
Special Chief Secretary to Government (FAC).

GOVERNMENT OF ANDHRA PRADESH

Office Of The Commissioner And Inspector General
Of Registration And Stamps, A.P., Vijayawada,

Circular Memo No: CARD/1819237/2023. Dated: 20/04/2023

Sub: Registration and Stamps Dept.. Lodging of Grievance IT based application
developed Instructions issued Regarding.

OK

Attention is invited to the subject cited. An IT based application for Grievance
Module was developed. The user can fill the application with details like use information
SRO, Grievance classification, filling the grievance description and upload the required
documents/proofs online (only in JPG/JPEG/PNG/PDF format with maximum size of SMB
only). On clicking submit button a Grievance ID will be generated and an E-mail will be
sent to the user with details of the Grievance to track the status the Grievance.

At the Officer end, Officer can view the details of the grievance, view and download the
attachments. Officer can reply to the grievance and attach any documents if needed. User
gets a mail regarding the reply sent. If user is not satisfied with the reply, he can appeal
to the next higher authority. In case if the officer does not reply to the grievance within 15
days of submission, it will be auto escalated to higher authority and the grievance will be
redressed to the user by higher authority.

 
& ° .».»”»+x,x}TE 7

In this regard, it is hereby directed that all the Grievance applications through the IT
based application must be attended.

 

The Url and login credentials will be intimated to all the Officers. The user manual is
provided in the website registration.ap.gov.in.

All the Officers are directed to strictly follow the above instructions. All the DIGS &
DRS are requested to see that the above instructions are implemented without fail.

Encl: User manual

V Rama Krishna
Commissioner and Inspector General of
Registration and Stamps, A.P., Vilayawada.

THE ANDHRA PRADESH
GAZETTE
PART IV-B EXTRAORDINARY
PUBLISHED BY AUTHORITY
ANDHRA PRADESH ACTS, ORDINANCES AND REGULATIONS Etc.,

The following Act of the Andhra Pradesh Legislature received the assent of the
Governor on the 19th April, 2023 and the said assent is hereby first published on the 24
April, 2023 in the Andhra Pradesh Gazette for general information:

ACT No. 7 of 2023

AN ACT FURTHER TO AMEND THE ANDHRA PRADESH RIGHTS IN LAND AND
PATTADAR PASS BOOKS ACT, 1971.

Be it enacted by the Legislature of the State of Andhra Pradesh in the Seventy-fourth
Year of the Republic of India as follows:-

1. Short Title and Commencement

(1) This Act may be called the Andhra Pradesh Rights in Land and Pattadar Pass
Books (Amendment) Act, 2023.

(2) It shall be deemed to have come into force with effect on and from the 1
February, 2023.

  
ly !

2. Amendment of Section 2. Act 26 of 1971

In the Andhra Pradesh Rights in Land and Pattadar Pass Books Act, 1971, (herein
after referred to as the principal Act), in section 2,-

(i) after clause (1)(f), the following shall be added, namely,-

"Certificate of Ownership" means the certificate of Ownership issued under Sec
6-A for

1. The lands located in the Gramakantam and non- agricultural lands in the village
and/or

2. Lands located in the Urban Local Bodies.".
(ii) The existing clause (4a) shall be renumbered as clause (4b)

(iii) Before clause (4b), as so numbered, the following clause shall be inserted
namely-

""Land in Urban Local Body" means land, including building thereon.
agricultural and non-agricultural lands located within the limits of the urban
local body.

Explanation:- The term Building shall be assigned the same meaning as
contained under respective enactments under which Urban Local Bodies are
constituted.".

(iv)After clause (11), the following shall be inserted namely,

11a). "Urban Local Body" means the areas comprising of “Municipality” "Nagar
Panchayat" "Local Area" "Transitional Area" as defined in Andhra Pradesh
Municipalities Act, 1965 and the area comprising of "Larger Urban Area"
"Corporation" as defined in Andhra Pradesh Municipal Corporations Act,
1994, Vijayawada Municipal Corporation Act, 1981 and Visakhapatnam
Municipal Corporation Act, 1979.".

3. Amendment of section 3(B).
In the principal Act, after section 3A, the following shall be added, namely, -

“3B. The procedure prescribed under sub-section (1) and (2) of section 3 Mutatis
Mutandis shall be followed for preparation and updating of Record of Rights in all lands in
Urban Local bodies and as further prescribed."

4. In the principal Act, in section 4, after sub-section (3), the following shall be
added, namely,-

(4) Acquisition of rights in land in urban local body to be intimated: The acquisition
of rights in respect of ownership of land in Urban Local Body shall be intimated
to the Tahsildar within Sixty (60) days from the date of such acquisition
and the said Tahsildar shall give or send a written acknowledgement of the
receipt of such intimation to the person making it.".

———

 
 

&  ° .».».”»”»+x,x}LEEZ 7 Ts

5. Amendment of section 6:

 

In the principal Act, in section 6A, after sub-section (5), the following shall be
added, namely, -

6A (6). Any person claiming title deed cum pass book of any agricultural
land or certificate of Ownership of non- agricultural land and buildings in the
limits of urban local body shall make the application as prescribed in Section
6A (1) and 6A (2), which shall be acted upon in the manner prescribed under
the Act and the Rules."

6A (7). An appeal against any order passed by the Tahsildar under this
Section lies to the concerned Revenue Divisional officer within Thirty (30)
days of the date of communication of the order.".

6. Amendment of section 9:

In the Principal Act, in Section 9, after the expression "sections 3,5,5A or 5B", the
expression "and 6A" shall be added.

7. Amendment of section 11(2) (bb):

In the principal Act, in section 11 (2)(bb), after the words "title deeds and pass
books" the words "and certificate of ownership" shall be added.

8. In the principal Act, after section 11, the following shall be added, namely, -

"11A. Notwithstanding anything contrary contained in any law for the time being
in force governing the urban and rural local bodies in the State, the provisions of
the Act, shall apply in regard to matters specifically provided for under this Act.".

9. Repeal and saving Ordinance No. 2 of 2023 :

(1) The Andhra Pradesh Rights in Land and Pattadar Pass Books (Amendment)
Ordinance, 2023, is hereby repealed.

(2) Notwithstanding such repeal, anything done or any action taken under the
said Ordinance shall be deemed to have been done or taken under this Act.

G. SATYA PRABHAKARA RAO,
Secretary to Government,
Legal and Legislative Affairs & Justice,
Law Department.

Office Of The Commissioner And Inspector General
Of Registration And Stamps, A.P., Vijayawada.

Circular Memo No. IAR/8575/2023 Date:25.04.2023

Sub: Internal Audit- Registration and Stamps Department- study on various points
review on performance of Internal Audit- re- examination of existing procedure
and instructions on internal audit- certain further comprehensive instructions
on conduct of internal audit-issued-Reg.

Ref: 1.The C&IG..(R&S), Procgs No.MVS/20588/07, Dated:24.1.2008
2. The C &IG., (R&S) Procgs No.IA/19019/09, Dated:28.7.2009
3. The C&IG., (R&S), Memo No.IA/4791/10. Dated: 1.4.2010. .
4. The C &IG., (R&S), Memo No.IA/8575/A/2014, Dated: 24.4.2015
5. The C&lG., (R&S), Circular Memo No.1A/117/2021, Dated: 1.11.21

OK

The immediate attention of all the officers in the department is invited to the subject
and reference cite. During the reviews on the performance of the Internal audit, it has
come to the notice of the Commissioner and Inspector General of Registration and
Stamps that, the performance of internal audit is not effective and instructions issued
from time to time on internal audit system in the State are not being adhered to. It is also
noticed that, no common procedure is being followed by the audit teams while conducting
audit and submission of reports. Therefore, in continuation to the existing procedure and
instructions issued in this matter. comprehensive further instructions are being issued
hereunder.

1. The audit of an SRO in a particular month by District registrar/Sub-Registrar (Market
Value & Audit) shall cover following documents:

a. 1st 150 sale deeds of high value in case of A-Category offices(as defined in
G.0.Ms No 638 Revenue (Registration-1) department), and 1 100 such sale deeds
in case of offices other than A-Category offices;

b. 30% of Gift deeds, Settlement deeds of higher value;
10% of Mortgage deeds;

d. 100% of the documents registered under "Anywhere registration shall also be
covered.

e.. All Partition deeds and Development Agreements;

f. All miscellaneous documents relating to immovable properties registered in
Book-1;

g. All documents where stamp duty is denoted u/s 16 of Indian Stamp Act, 1899;

 
 

All documents registered in post-manual option;
All documents allowed exemption/concession of stamp duty and fee;
All documents registered in Book-lll and Book-lV.

h.
L.
J.
k.

All documents for which grant was given to the properties involved in prohibited
list, but admitted under specific reason.

1.1 Further the following records shall be Verified at the time of audit:

a) Verification of Registration of Government land/Assigned land/ wakf land
etc. prohibited u/s 22-A of the Registration Act, 1908,

b) Verification of Web-land data.

c) Verification of DD/Pay Order for registered documents.

d) Verification of Pending documents and reasons for pendency.

e) Verification of rescanning and index editing, whether done under proper
authorization.

f) Test check of Transfer Duty allocation and period up to which adjustment is
completed.

g) Verification of stock and sales of stamp counter.

h) Verification of self-certification by Sub-Registrars/DDOs regarding
reconciliation of receipts and expenditure with CFMS/e-stamp. This shall
reflect as one of the item saying which item reconciled up to which month
and fact of tally or discrepancy if any.

Note: The verification related to serial no.s a) and b) of the above shall be limited to

the documents prescribed for audit for the respective month of concerned Sub Registrar
Office concerned.

2.

The audit party shall write the audit para, clearly showing as to how the loss of
revenue has occurred supported by the act and rules, guidelines and instructions
issued from time to time. The name of the Sub-Registrar is responsible for revenue
loss who register the document shall be written clearly under the remark.

The audit officers are informed that the system of internal audit (which is post audit)
is introduced solely to unearth cases wherein stamp duty was not paid on the market
value as per the guidelines and adopting proper classification and hence the audit
must be effective and purposeful. Any false report or omission to report the lapses
of Sub- registrars by the audit parties will lead to disciplinary cases.

The District Registrar/Sub-Registrar (MV&Audit) shall make sustainable remarks
supported by provisions of Act, Rules and instructions issued from time to time.

The Deputy Inspector General concerned shall also pay attention and review orders
on audit reports and ensure non-recurrence of unsustainable remarks by Audit
officers, by giving suitable instructions at the time of Annual Inspection of the office
of DR (MV&Audit) concerned without fail.

 

 

 
 

 

In respect of A & B category offices with arrears of audit for more than 6. months,
the Deputy Inspector General shall constitute special teams duly adjusting the man
power and ensure that the arrears of audit are cleared off expeditiously.

The District Registrar/Sub-Registrar(Market Value and Audit) on completion of the
audit of an office shall prepare the audit report in the standard format prescribed
(Annexure |) within 3(three) days and forward the physical copy of the same to the
Sub-Registrar concerned in addition to the soft copy through igrs e-mail also. The
Jurisdictional Sub-Registrar shall re-submit the audit report with his explanations
within (7) days. After submission of audit report with explanations, the District
Registrar/Sub-Registrar (MV&Audit) shall submit the audit report to the Deputy
Inspector General within (3) days by adding his further remarks if any. If the Sub-
Registrar who registered the document is in another station due to transfer or
promotion, District Registrar (MV&A)/Sub Registrar (MV&A) shall sends a scanned
copy of the report to the said Sub- Registrar at his present address through mail or
any communication channel. The explanations of the Sub-Registrar in the above case
shall be obtained and the audit report with answers on all paras shall be submitted
within (15) days from the date of receipt of audit report physically or through mail
or any mode of transmission. In case of death of such a Sub-Registrar concerned,
the present Sub-Registrar shall answer the audit remark. In case the Sub- Registrar/
in-charge Sub-Registrar concerned is retired from service, the District Registrar
(MV&A)/Sub Registrar (MV&A) has to serve a copy of the audit report along with
all connected material to the retired Sub-Registrar under proper acknowledgement.
The time limit for receipt of answers by the retired employee shall be (15) days.
If the audit report is not re-submitted by the Sub-Registrar along with explanation
within the above time, it shall be deemed as answered and the District Registrar/
Sub-Registrar (M.V.& Audit) shall proceed to add further remarks and submit the
same to the Deputy Inspector General

Soon after the receipt of the audit report with further remarks of the District Registrar/
Sub-Registrar (M.V.&Audit), the Deputy Inspector General shall pass final orders in
respect of accepted paras. The DIG shall report the contested paras to be placed
before the subsequent MMC for a decision. (DIGs shall refer such contested paras to
MMC where he/she is unable to pass final orders duly citing specific reasons). Priority
shall be given to place the contested paras by employees already retired or going to be
retired in the coming months. After issue of final orders in respect of accepted paras
or on receipt of a decision by the MMC, the DIG shall issue instructions to the District
Registrar concerned to take prompt follow up action to recover the loss, if any, from
the parties concerned for recovery of the deficit amounts under relevant statutory
provisions. A copy of the review shall be sent to the Sub-Registrar concerned along
with duplicate copy of audit report received from the District Registrar/Sub-Registrar
(Market Value and Audit).

District Registrar/Sub Registrar (Market Value and Audit) shall maintain a register
called "Register of Internal Audit Reports” in the proforma (Annexure-ll). Every audit
report must be accounted for in this register. All the previous audit reports shall also

 
 

10.

11.

12.

be assigned serial numbers and accounted for in this register. In all correspondence
related to audit, the serial number of the audit report and serial number of audit Para
must be referred to. When action is finalized against any audit Para, a suitable entry
shall be made in the audit report against the para and the number of the para shall
be rounded off in red ink. When action on all the paras of the audit reports is finalized
the audit report file shall be closed and its number rounded off in the register in red
ink.

In order to review the performance of the District Registrar/Sub Registrar (Market
Value and Audit) and to assess the progress made in the matter of collection of deficit
stamp duty and Registration fee a monthly return in the form (Annexure-lll) shall be
submitted to the Deputy Inspector General by the District Registrar/Sub Registrar
Market Value and Audit) by 5th of succeeding month. The Deputy Inspectors General
shall review this item with regular District Registrars and especially on status of
audit, collections and further action.

District Registrar/Sub Registrar(Market Value and Audit), while drawing up their
programmes of Audit, must ensure that back log is cleared before taking up fresh
cases. In case any Sub-Registrar is going to retire, the audit of that office shall be taken
up and completed and report shall be issued upto the end of the month preceding
his retirement. The audit of the last month also shall be completed before the end
of 1 week of the succeeding month. In case any Sub- Registrar is allowed to retire
without audit being completed upto the end of the preceding month of his retirement,
it shall be reflected against the CR of the Audit officer concerned. A statement with
regard to status of audit in the SROS where the Sub-Registrar is going to be retired
in that year shall be submitted to the Deputy Inspector General in Annexure-lV

The District Registrar(Market Value & Audit) shall collect all the deficit amount up
to Rs.20,000/- in each document from the parties concerned under section 41-A of
Indian Stamp Act in the paras accepted by the Sub-Registrar concerned or finalized
by the DIG/MMC. If the amount of loss is more than Rs 20,000/- in each document,
the District Registrar(Regular) of the District concerned has to collect the deficit
amount under Section 41-A of Indian Stamp Act on receipt of review orders from the
Deputy Inspector General concerned. Within 15 days of receiving of final orders from
either DIG or MMC, the District Registrar (Market Value & Audit)/ District Registrar
(regular) is required to issue a notice under Section 41-A of the Indian Stamp Act,
1899. Final orders must be passed within two months in non-contested paras and
within three months in contested paras. Once final orders are passed, Sub-registrars
shall record the details of deficit amounts in the relevant indexes. A copy of the final
orders and index entry must be submitted to the Deputy Inspector General without
fail. The District Registrar(Regular) and District Registrar (MV&A) shall submit a
monthly report in this regard to the Dy. Inspector General in Annexure-V. Where the
District Registrar (MV&A) post does not exists and the audit unit is headed by Sub-
Registrar(Market Value & Audit), the District Registrar (Regular) has to collect the
deficit amounts from the parties concerned in all accepted paras and paras finalized
by the Deputy Inspector General/MMC concerned (i.e. in below and above Rs.20000
cases).

 

 

 
 

13. The Deputy Inspector General concerned shall submit details to the Commissioner

14.

15.

& Inspector General (R&S) along with the review orders where the deficit amount
exceeds Rs.1,00,000/- per document caused due to deliberate/intentional lapse of
Sub- Registrar in respect of A&B category offices and where the deficit amount is
Rs.50,000/- and more in respect of other offices after initiating disciplinary action
against such Sub-Registrars who deliberately/intentionally responsible for loss
of Revenue. Soon after finalization of Audit reports/paras by the Deputy Inspector
General/MMC, the Deputy Inspector General concerned shall issue instructions to the
District Registrar concerned to prompt follow up action to recover the loss from the
parties concerned under section 41A and wherever necessary, take further action
under section 48 of Indian stamp Act, 1899. The District Registrars/Deputy Inspectors
General shall maintain data base containing para wise, year wise and Sub- Registrar
Office information on all kinds of audit paras and information on the action taken
audit paras including disciplinary action. The data base has to be updated by 10 of
every month. Every District Registrar and the Sub- Registrar shall open a register to
be called "Register of Internal Audit Paras” in proforma (Annexure VI).

The District Registrar immediately after collection of the deficit amount relating
to any audit para shall inform the Deputy Inspector General / District Registrar/Sub
Registrar (Market Value and Audit) and Sub- Registrar concerned so as to enable the
concerned to make an entry against the para and close it

The Deputy Inspector General concerned has to conduct Annual inspection of the
offices of District Registrar/Sub-Registrar(Market Value & Audit) in the Zone every
year and submit report to the Commissioner and Inspector General of Registration
and Stamps. A.P., Vijayawada along with his further remarks on the explanation of
the District Registrar/Sub-Registrar/Market Value & Audit) concerned for passing
final orders in the matter.

The Deputy Inspector General concerned shall review every month the progress
of Internal Audit and progress of collection of deficit amount in the finalized Audit
paras and ensure that the audit in A & B category offices is up to date and all deficit
amounts are collected within a period of 3 months from the date of communication
of review orders to the District Registrar concerned.

Every Deputy Inspector General must maintain a register called "Register of Internal

Audit Reports” in the proforma (Annexure VII) And the Deputy Inspectors General shall
submit a monthly return to the Commissioner and Inspector General of Registration and
Stamps by Sth of succeeding month in Annexure VIII & IX.

All the officers are instructed to follow the instructions issued strictly and any

deviation will be viewed seriously.

Encl. Annexure | to IX-

V Rama krishna
Commissioner and Inspector General of
Registration and Stamps, A.P., Vijayawada

Challan and Remittance.

 

 

 

Regarding file of Aadhar Copies Parties and Scribe:

The Director and Inspector General of Registration and Stamps, A.P. Vijayawada

at point Nos.C and D in Memo.No.G1/8686/2015, Dated 04.09.2018 categorically

mentioned that

1) The Photostat copy of the Aadhar ID submitted along with the document
by the Executant, Claimant/representatives and witnesses need to be filed
separately and should not be scanned with the document.

2) The Photostat copy of the scribe 's Aadhar ID proof shall be filed separately
but shall not be mentioned in document or scanned document. But in general,
the Sub Registrar is not followed the above instructions. Hence, informed to
follow the instructions without fail.

ABSTRACT

Registration & Stamps Department - CARD Project - Enhancement of User Charges for
registration of certain documents - Orders - Issued.

REVENUE (REGISTRATION.|) DEPARTMENT
G.0.Ms.No.192 Dated:25.04.2023
Read the following:
1. G.0.Ms.No.576, Revenue (Regn.1) Department, dt.29-08-2001.

2. From the Commissioner & Inspector General (R&S), A.P Vijayawada, Lr.No.CARD/e-
1373901/2021,Dt:01/11/2021.

ORDER:

In the G.O. first read above, permission was accorded to the Commissioner and
Inspector General, Registration and Stamps for levying service charges to be
collected from citizens for issue of certain certificates and for registration of certain
documents etc., through CARD.

2. The Commissioner and Inspector General, Registration and Stamps, Andhra Pradesh,
Vijayawada, in his letter second read above, has proposed for revision of user charges
in view of escalation of day to day expenditure in providing computerized services
to the public as the prices of inputs such as consumables, hardware, networking
equipment, power back-up arrangements and electricity bills which are fixed long
back based on the expenditure at that time.

  
 

 

3. Government, after careful examination of the proposal, hereby order the levy of the
enhanced User Charges on the services shown below against each:-

4 Wav ose) mentioned in Book-l, 100, 200 500

Sale of Stamps

4. These orders shall come into force with immediate effect.
5. This order issues with the concurrence of the Finance Department vide their U.0. No.
FMUDASD(REV)/182/2021, dated 20.12.2021 Computer No. 1572248.

6. The Commissioner and Inspector General, Registration and Stamps, Andhra Pradesh,
Vijayawada is requested to take action accordingly.

Office Of The Commissioner And Inspector General

Circular Memo No.GS01/15423/2008 Dated:08.05.2023.

Sub: Registration and Stamps Department e- Stamping -SHCIL appointed CRA/
Nodal agency for e-stamping operations Appointment of ACCS - Operational
guidelines - Issued.

Ref: G.0.Ms.No.103, Revenue(Regn-1) Dept, Dt:07.03.2023.

OK

1) E-stamping is a computer based application and a secured electronic way of
stamping documents. The prevailing system of physical stamp paper/franking
is being replaced by e-stamping system. SHCIL is the only CRA ( Central Record
keeping Agency) appointed by the Government of India. Central Record Keeping
Agency is responsible for User Registration, imprest Balance Administration and
overall e-Stamping Application Operations and Maintenance. SHCIL who is also CRA
will appoint ACCS (Authorized Collection centers) who will issue certificates to the
clients at their counters. ACCS act as the intermediary between the SHCIL and stamp
duty payer.

2) Since the ‘e-Stamping’ model is all set to roll out across the state, it has become
imperative to draw a comprehensive operational procedure as to various aspects
concerning ACCs viz.,

3) Types of ACCs and their appointment:

a) Individuals: As regards the appointment of individuals as ACCs, the qualifications
and other criteria as are followed for issue of licenses to the Stamp vendors
shall be followed mutatis mutandis.

b) Existing Stamp Vendors: In respect of incumbent Stamp Vendors, their
appointment can be considered as ACC, subject to adherence of the terms and
conditions stipulated in this regard.

c) Financial and other recognized institutions: With regard to the Financial
Institutions, Recognized Insurance Companies and other categories of institutions
as are mentioned in Para 3.2 of the Agreement between CCRA and CRA, the CRA
shall devise a mechanism for appointing them as ACCs.

4) The vital parameters like qualification, domain experience in rendering the service
and the antecedents of the applicant shall be looked into before selection and
appointment of an ACC. Final call for appointment of ACCS amongst the applicants in
a particular locality is vested with CRA i.e., SHCIL.

  
 

5)

6)

7)

8)

9)

10)

11

 

Modalities of work place: All ACCS shall operate in a web based environment and
shall adhere to the specifications of network connectivity, equipment and other
protocols as mandated by the SHCIL.

Qualification: Qualification for individuals who apply for appointment as ACC shall be
a pass in SSC or 10th class. Antecedents of the individual have to be checked before
appointing the individual as ACC. Character and Conduct certificate of the individuals
shall also be verified. A declaration from the individual to the effect that he is not of
unsound mind and not involved in any case of moral turpitude and is not an adjudged
insolvent, shall also be taken.

Jurisdiction of the License: The appointee as ACC shall do the activity within the
jurisdiction of the SRO, where he is appointed, and shall at no cost operate beyond
his assigned jurisdiction.

Supervision over ACCS: ACCs shall strictly adhere to the stipulations of appointment.
Appointment can be cancelled upon breach of conditions/stipulations contained in
the agreement by the CRA. CRA shall exercise supervision over the ACCS through
Its authorized staff in each District. They should verify the efficacy of the system, its
functioning and such other things as are warranted under the terms of agreement
between ACC and the CRA.

Reconciliations of Receipts: CRA shall remit all the consolidated amounts of the
Stamp Duty collected by way of cash or electronic modes to the prescribed Head
of Account, after the deduction of the commission it is entitled to. It shall be done
within the time allowed as per the agreement, depending on the mode of receipt or
collection of Stamp Duty. The CRA shall maintain daily account of such remittances
in the register as prescribed in the Form-é. The receipts shall be reconciled with the
remittances on such periodicity as may be agreed by both CCRA and CRA.

Maintenances of Registers: The ACCS( all categories) shall maintain registers as are
prescribed for the purpose as specified under AP Stamp(Payment of Duty by means
of e-Stamping) Rules, 2016. The feasibility of maintenance of registers in electronic
form shall also be studied in view of their easy access and instant transmit across
the board.

Responsibility of CRA: The CRA shall provide uninterrupted service to the ACCs. The
payment services shall also be made available to the citizens. The CRA has to provide
grievance redressal mechanism to citizens. For any kind of dispute that may arise
between CRA and ACC, CRA is shall take the wholesome responsibility to address the
dispute.

V Rama krishna
Commissioner and Inspector General of
Registration and Stamps, A.P., Vijayawada.

Sub: Registration and Stamps Department - Chit Funds Act, 1982 Implementation
of new application "Chit Fund Regulatory and Management System- CFRMS"in
the day to day operations relating to chit business w.e.f. 15.05.2023 Reg.

OK

Attention of all the officers noted in the address entry is invited to the subject cited.
It is informed that a new application software to deal with all the chit transactions i.e.,
issuance of PSO, Commencement of Chits, Filing of Minutes, etc., both at Chit Fund Unit
end as well as the Department Officers end has been developed and is being launched on
15.05.2023. The detailed user manual showing the procedures to be followed in dealing
with the aforesaid chit services is enclosed herewith along with address of the website
(Url :https://echits.rs.ap.gov.in).

Therefore, all the Officers are requested to conduct all transactions related to chit
fund only through CFRMS Module. All the new chit transactions like PSO & Commencement
shall be only through CFRMS.

However, the ongoing transactions relating to chit groups already commenced may
be continued in physical mode till the data porting is completed.

Encl: User Manual.\

GOVERNMENT OF ANDHRA PRADESH
ABSTRACT

Public Services - Human Resources - Transfers and Postings of Employees - Guidelines /
Instructions - Orders - Issued.

FINANCE (HR.I-PLG. & POLICY) DEPARTMENT
G.0.Ms.No.71 Dated: 17-05-2023.

 
 

Read the following:-

. G.0.Ms.No.98. Finance (HR.|) Department, Dt.04-08-2015.

. G.0.Ms.No. 140, Finance (HR.1) Department, Dt. 16-11-2015.

. G.0.Ms.No. 102, Finance (HR.I-Plg. & Policy) Department, Dt. 10-06-2016.
. G.0.Ms.No. 123, Finance (HR.I-Plg. & Policy) Department, Dt.28-06-2016.
. G.0.Ms.No.64, Finance (HR.I-Plg. & Policy) Department, Dt.21-04-2017.

. G.0.Ms.No.72, Finance (HR.I-Plg. & Policy) Department, Dt.05-05-2017.

. G.0.Ms.No.75, Finance (HR.1-Plg. & Policy) Department, Dt.09-05-2017.

. G.0.Ms.No.57, Finance (HR.1-Plg. & Policy) Department, Dt.08-05-2018.
9. G.0.Ms.No.45, Finance (HR.I-Plg. & Policy) Department, Dt.24-06-2019.
10. G.0.Ms.No.59, Finance (HR.I-Plg. & Policy) Department, Dt.04-07-2019.
11. G.0.Ms.No.116, Finance (HR.1-Plg. & Policy) Department, Dt.07-06-2022.
12. G.0.Ms.No. 122, Finance (HR.I-Plg. & Policy) Department, Dt. 16-06-2022.

ORDER:

The Government is committed to the welfare of its employees and seeks to promote
work-life balance to enable the employees and their families lead happy and
contented lives. In this direction, the Government's policy is to ensure that every
employee is posted at a work place where she/he is most productive and contributes
his/her best for improved governance and efficient delivery public services. Further,
overall, the Government is committed to right placement of the staff to improve
efficiency and accountability in administration.

ONOTRWNE

2. Accordingly, in super session of the orders issued in the references 11th & 12th
read above, the Government hereby issue the following guidelines for transfer of
employees for the year 2023.

3. The existing ban on transfer of employees imposed in the references 11th & 12th read
above is relaxed for the period from 22 May to 31" May to ensure right placement
of employees to secure optimum productivity and commitment to furtherance of
Government's development objectives.

Principles for Transfers and Postings:
The following principles may be adopted while effecting transfer of employees:
i. Transfers shall be effected only "on request" basis and on administrative grounds.

ii. Employees who completed 2 years of service at a station as on 30th April,
2023 are only eligible for request transfers and those who completed 5 years of
service at a station as on said date, shall be invariably transferred.

iii. Service in all cadres at a station shall be counted while calculating the period of
stay. Station means place (City, Town, Village) of actual working for the purpose
of transfers and not office or institution.

 

 

 
i —

vi.

vii.

viii.

xi.

 

However, for State Audit Department, station means Office within the Zone as all
their offices are situated in the District Headquarters only.

Preference will be given to below categories:

a. Employees with disabilities of 40% or more as certified by a competent
authority as per the norms of "persons with disabilities".

b. Employees having mentally challenged children to a place where medical
facilities are available.

c. Medical grounds for the diseases (either self or spouse or dependent children
and dependent parents) of Cancer, Open Heart Operations, Neuro Surgery,
Kidney Transplantation to places where such facilities are available.

d. Widow employee appointed on Compassionate appointments.

e. Husband and Wife cases (only one of the spouses shall be shifted following
the prescribed procedure). Once the facility is utilized, the next request can
be made only after five years.

All transfers affected by following the procedure where employees indicated
preference for stations shall be treated as request transfers for the purpose of
sanction of TTA and other transfer benefits.

Employees shall invariably be transferred from their existing location on
promotion unless no such posts exist at a different location.

All vacancies in Notified Agency areas shall be filled first before filling posts in
the non-ITDA areas.

The employees (Local Cadres, Zonal Cadres) working in ITDA areas for more than
two years may be transferred to the place of their choice subject to fulfillment
of conditions stipulated in this GO, with due preference to the inter-se seniority
among the employees working in these areas.

For the purpose of posting in ITDA Areas the following shall be the criteria:
1. The employees below 50 years of age.

2. The employees who have not worked earlier in the ITDA areas so far based
on the length of service in plain area downwards.

Besides ITDA areas, areas which are interior and backward with large number
of vacancies shall be given preference while filling up of vacancies on transfers.
HoDs and District Collectors shall ensure the same.
 

Procedure for Transfers:

10.

a) The relaxation on transfers shall be effective from 22 May to 31” May 2023.

b)  Allthe transfers shall be effected by the competent authorities as per the existing
orders of delegation subject to the existing Government Orders and conditions
prescribed,

c) The Head of the Department concerned is responsible for the implementation
of the transfer orders in the most transparent manner possible without giving
any scope for complaints / allegations. Any violation of these guidelines will be
viewed seriously,

The following departments, which have unique operational systems may devise their
own transfer guidelines relevant to their Departments subject to following the above
guidelines. They include the revenue earning departments viz., i) Commercial Taxes;
ii) Prohibition & Excise; iii) Stamps & Registration; iv) Transport Department, and v)
Agriculture Department. They shall also complete the process by 31" May 2023.

The Education Departments viz., School Education, Higher Education, Intermediate
Education, Technical Education and the Education Departments working under the
Welfare Departments are exempted from the above transfer guidelines and they may
also devise their own transfer guidelines relevant to their Departments with the prior
approval of Finance Department.

The standing instructions on the transfers of office bearers of recognized Employees
Associations as issued in Circular Memo No. GADO1-SWOSERA/27/2019-SW, GA
(Services Welfare) Department, dt. 15.06.2022 will apply i.e., not to transfer the
office bearers of all the Recognized Service Associations in the State at State level,
District level & Division/Taluk level until their completion of three (3) terms or nine
(9) years of stay in a particular station.

The visually challenged employees are exempted from transfers except when
they make a specific request for transfer. As far as possible, these categories of
employees may be posted at a place of their choice subject to availability of a clear
vacancy.

The requests of the employees having any charges /ACB/Vigilance cases pending
against him/her shall not be considered for transfer. The Authority shall indicate the
fact clearly against the name of that employee if there is any request for transfer.

The ban on transfers shall come into force with effect from 01.06. 2023.
A copy of this order is available at http://apegazette.cgg.gov.in.
(BY ORDER AND IN THE NAME OF THE GOVERNOR OF ANDHRA PRADESH)

SHAMSHER SINGH RAWAT
SPECIAL CHIEF SECRETARY TO GOVERNMENT
