Office of the Chief Commissioner of Land
Administration. A.P., Mangalagiri, Guntur District.

CCLA's Ref. No.Assn.1(1)/351/2022, dated.20/05/2022
CIRCULAR

Sub: Lands Assignment of land to the Political Sufferers / Freedom Fighters
Requests for deletion from the list of prohibited properties notified u/S.22-A(1)
of Registration Act, 1908- Further instructions / clarification - Sought for-
Issued -Regarding.

Ref: 1.G.0.Ms.No.1743, Revenue Department, dated 28.08.1959.
2.G.0.Ms.No.946, Revenue (B) Department, dated 26.7.1979.
3.G.0.Ms.No.472, Revenue (Assignment-1) Department, dated 22.5.1993.
4.G.0.Ms.No.185, Revenue (Assignment-1) Department, dated 11.3.1997.
5.G.0.Ms.No.1045, Revenue (Assignment-1) Department, dated 15.12.2004.
6.G.0.Ms.No.181, Revenue (Assignment-1) Department, dated 15.2.2006.
7.G.0.Ms.No.307, Revenue (Assn.1) Department, dated 06.06.2013.
8.G.0.Ms.No.279, Revenue(Assn.1) Department, dated 04.07.2016.
9.Collector, Visakhapatnam Rc.No. 404/2022/E3(Land), dated 21.04.2022.
10.CCLA's Circular Instructions Ref. No.L.R.-II(1)/551/2022, dated 25.04.2022.
11.CCLA's Circular Instructions Ref. No.Assn.1(1)/350/2022, dated 04.05.2022.

OK

| invite attention of the District Collectors in the State to the reference last cited,
wherein the District Collector, Visakhapatnam has sought for certain clarifications on
lands assigned to Political Sufferers / Freedom Fighters and their requests for deletion of
such assigned lands from the prohibitory list notified under Sec.22-A(1) of Registration
Act, 1908.

In this context, it is informed that the Government have over a period of time
assigned agriculture lands to Political Sufferers, Freedom Fighters for their livelihood. The

  
SS ee)

Government have stipulated that lands assigned to Freedom Fighters by the Government,
shall not be sold or otherwise alienated for a period of ten years from the date of
assignment.

After the examining the issues raised by the Collector Visakhapatnam, on assignment
of lands to Political Sufferers / Freedom Fighters, the following circular instructions/
clarifications are hereby issued.

1. PASSBOOKS TO THE EXISTING ASSIGNEES UNDER POLITICAL SUFFERERS/
FREEDOM FIGHTERS QUOTA:

The approval of the Collector is mandatory before issuing of Pattadar Pass Book by
the Tahsildar to any political sufferer/freedom fighter, in relation to land that has been
assigned to him prior to these instructions.

2. PROCESSING OF FILES ONLY IN E-OFFICE:

All files regarding issue of Pattadar Pass Book by the Tahsildar to any political
sufferers/freedom fighters, shall be only through the e-office system. Physical files shall
not be processed.

3. IDENTITY OF POLITICAL SUFFERERS/FREEDOM FIGHTERS:

In every case of deletion of land from the prohibitory list notified under Sec.22-A of
Registration Act, 1908 or in cases disputes/court cases:

@ The Collector shall essentially cause verification of the credentials of the
political sufferers/freedom fightersby using the Gazette Notifications issued by
the Government with the names of the political sufferers/freedom fighters

@ Acopy of the Gazette Notification shall be attached/placed in the e-file of the
matter (in Correspondence section of the e-office file).

@ In all cases of doubts regarding veracity of the political sufferers/freedom
fighters, the matter may be thoroughly verified from different records that may
be available.

4, FILE MISSING CASES:

In case, the original DR file is missing, Collector shall ask the political sufferers/
freedom fighters to provide evidence of them being the political sufferer/freedom fighter.
The District Collector shall cause the information provided to be cross verified with existing
alternate records to confirm the veracity of the paper submitted.

5. AP ASSIGNED LANDS (PROHIBITION OF TRANSFERS) ACT, 1977 NOT TO APPLY:

it is clarified that in cases of assignment made to the political sufferers/freedom
fighters it shall be presumed that the assignee political sufferer/freedom fighter is NOT a
"landless poor person" for the purposes of the AP Assigned Lands (Prohibition of Transfers)
Act, 1977.

 
 

Hence, no assignment made to the political sufferers/freedom fighters, at any point
of time, shall attract the provisions of the AP Assigned Lands (Prohibition of Transfers) Act,
1977. However, it is reiterated that no alienation is permitted in case of lands assigned to
freedom fighters within ten (10) years of assignment.

All assignments made to the freedom fighters, at whatever point of time they have
been made, shall be alienable Ten (10) years after the date of assignment.

In case the patta was purported to have been issued by mistake of fact and
misrepresentation, the Tahsildars shall, under such circumstances, take action to cancel
the assignment and resume the land to the Government. In all such cases, the affected
individuals shall be given an opportunity of being heard.

There shall be no limitation in this matter and action shall be initiated as soon as
such facts come to the notice of the Tahsildar.

6. SALE OF LAND ASSIGNED TO POLITICAL SUFFERER AND FREEDOM FIGHTER:

In case of Political Sufferers, the Government have issued orders that the political
sufferers are permitted to sell away the lands assigned to them without imposing any
conditions vide G.O.Ms.No. 1743 Revenue Department, dated.28.8.1959.

In case of Freedom Fighters, the Government have issued orders that they shall not
sell or otherwise alienate the lands assigned to them for a period of ten (10) years vide
G.0.Ms.No.1045, Revenue (Assn.1) Department dated 15.12.2004.

7. NO NOC REQUIRED TO SELL ASSIGNED LANDS BY THE FREEDOM FIGHTERS
AFTER TEN YEARS;

The Government have issued orders vide G.O.Ms.No.279, Revenue (Assignment.1)
Department dated 04.07.2016 dispensing with the procedure of issuing NOCS: that "there
shall be no need for obtaining NOC in all cases of assignment of freedom fighters in which
a period of ten (10) years has been completed and there is no dispute on the land with the
Government".

@ The lands assigned to freedom fighters may be kept in separate annexure of
prohibitory list under Sec. 22-A of Registration Act, 1908 with date of assignment
certified by the District Collector, with a clause that all the assigned lands to the
freedom fighters shall stand withdrawn from the prohibitory list notified from Sec.
22-A of Registration Act, 1908 on completion of ten (10) years of Assignment. This
will obviate the need for NOC to sell the lands assigned under freedom fighters
category.

@ = lf there is any explicit dispute over the genuinity of the assignment itself, or the
genuinity of the freedom fighter, the District Collector shall address the Registration
Dept., separately not to entertain any registration on the said lands.

@ A provision may be made in webland to ensure that the lands assigned to freedom
fighters are deleted from assigned category after ten (10) years of Assignment.

 

 

 
Se

@ Incase of the lands assigned to the political sufferers may be kept in separate of
prohibitory list under Sec. 22-A of Registration Act.

1908 with the date of assignment and may be deleted from the prohibitory list notified
under Sec. 22-A of Registration Act, 1908 and communicate the list to the District
Registrars and also to the Sub-Registrars concerned.

8. INCLUSION OF FREEDOM FIGHTERS ASSIGNED LANDS IN PROHBITORY LIST
UNDER SECTION 22-A(1)(e) OF REGISTRATION Act, 1908:

This issue is covered by orders already issued in G.0.Ms.No.279, Revenue
(Assignment.1) Department dated 04.07.2016.

Government have ordered that in all cases of assignment of freedom fighters in
which a period of ten (10) years has expired and there is no dispute on the land with
the Government shall be deleted from the prohibitory list notified under Sec. 22-A of
Registration Act, 1908 and furnished to the Registration Department.

Since the date of assignment is crucial for reckoning the ten (10) years period, all
assignment pattas shall be digitized and made available in webland for ready verification.

In respect of cases in which there is a dispute with Government about the genuineness
of the assignment or otherwise, a list of such cases shall be prepared by District Collector
and furnished to Registration Department by following the procedure under Sec. 22-A of
Registration Act, 1908. The Sub-Registrar shall enter the details of such disputed lands in
the online records deleting all other lands in which there is no dispute.

9. INCLUSION IN PROHBITORY LIST UNDER SECTION 22-A(1)(e) OF REGISTRATION
Act, 1908 ON IRRELEVANT GROUNDS:

No land assigned to any political sufferer shall be placed in prohibitory list under Sec.
22- A of Registration Act, 1908 except in cases or there are questions about genuinity of
the assignment itself. There shall be no other ground for placing such lands in Prohibitory
list under Sec. 22-A of Registration Act, 1908.

No land assigned to any freedom fighter shall be placed in prohibitory list under
Sec. 22-A Registration Act, 1908 except in cases where the period of 10 years is not
completed from the date of assignment and/or there are questions about genuinity of the
assignment itself. There shall be no other ground for placing such lands in prohibitory list
under Sec. 22-A of Registration Act, 1908.

10. APPEALS:

Any appeal against the orders of the Collector shall lie with the Commissioner of
Appeals. Final appeal against the orders of the Commissioner Appeals shall lie with the
Chief Commissioner of Land Administration.

ars

 
 

11. PURCHASERS OF POLITICAL SUFFERERS/FREEDOM FIGHTERS:

Several applications have been filed by 3rd parties who have purchased the lands
(hitherto given to political sufferers/freedom fighters) and are requesting for deletion of
such lands from the prohibitory list notified under Sec. 22-A of Registration Act, 1908.

In case of freedom fighters/political sufferers, the genuinity of the assignment may
be ensured by the Collector. If the assignment is genuine and the transaction had occurred
ten (10) years after the assignment, by way of a Registered Sale Deed, the same may be
deleted from the prohibitory list notified under Sec. 22-A of Registration Act, 1908. The
original assignee need not be consulted.

14. IRREGULAR ASSIGNMENT/BOGUS/FABRICATED PATTAS:

Whenever the cases of fabricated/forged/fake/bogus D-Form Pattas alleged to be
assigned to Political Sufferers/Freedom Fighters are noticed, the Collectors shall take
necessary action for cancellation of such Assignment pattas. Pattadar Passbook/Title
Deed if any issued in such fraudulent cases, Collector shall take necessary action for
cancellation under ROR Act and Rules.

In all such cases, a Criminal case shall be lodged without exception.
15. CLARIFICATIONS SOUGHT BY COLLECTOR, VISAKHAPATNAM:

Memo No. REV01-REGSOMISC/106/2023-REGN-| DI 31-5-2023,

Sub: Revenue (Registration and Stamps) Department Scheme Revision of Market
Value Guidelines Rules, 1998- Request to accord permission to take-up
Special Revision of Market Values both in Urban & Rural in certain areas and
Structure Values in Market Value the State w.e.f. 01-06-2023-Permission
accorded - Reg.

Ref: From C&G, R&S, Vijayawada, Letter No.MV1/752/2022, Dated: 29.05.2023.

OK

1. Government of Andhra Pradesh had issued Market Value Guidelines Rules, 1998 for
Revision of Market Values in the state from time to time. As per Rule 5 of said rules,
the periodicity of Revision is every year for Urban areas (under rule (2) (a)), every
alternative year for rural areas under rule (4 (2) (c)) and every year for structure
rates (under rule 4(2)(d)). Accordingly, in the reference cited, the C&IG. R&S, A.P. has
submitted the proposals to the Government to revise the Market Values of certain
areas under special revision (Rule 9) in the state where huge gap exists between the
open market values with that of Department guideline values and to implement the
revised values w.e.f. 01-06-2023. The C&IG, R&S, AP, Vijayawada has also requested
to accord permission to revise structure values in the state w.e.f 01-06-2023.

2. Government after careful examination of the matter hereby accord permission to the
Commissioner and Inspector General, R&S, AP to revise the Market Values of certain
areas in the State under special revision (Rule 9) where huge gap exists between the
open market values with that of Department guideline values and also to revise the
structure values in the state w.e.f 01-06-2023.

Dr. K.S. JAWAHAR REDDY,

in the document.

l.e., Panchayat Offices, Municipal Corporations etc. and filed in the separate file in

and Rs 20 shall be applicable for each floor. I.e., composite value + 30 for 3rd floor,
composite value + 60 for 4th floor and composite value + 90 for Sth floor so on for the
structures in Col No 2. The same analogy shall be followed for Col No 3 and 4 also.

(02) classification included in Form-ll.

Memo No. CARD/e-1786559/2023 Dt.15.06.2023

Sub: R&S, Dept. - Standard Operating Procedure for Delivery of Registration
services-LPM based registrations-At GSWS/VSWS-Reg.

OK

Kind attention is invited to the subject cited. The Standard Operating Procedure for
delivery of Registration services for LPM based at VSWS/GSWS is mentioned below:

1. The office of the Sub Registrar (GS & WS) shall guide the registering public to
approach survey/revenue department if the property requires sub-division prior to
registration as per the guidelines issued by the Government.

2. Registration Public/GSWS (SR) will make the data entry through PRIMME and submit
the document to the concerned SR along with PRIMME ID.

3. A duly executed document shall be presented before the Sub Registrar (GS&WS)
and the Sub Registrar (GS & WS) shall conduct the enquiry before registration of
the executing parties as to the factum of execution of the document, receipt of
consideration etc., and identifying witnesses to satisfy the identity of the executing
parties as required under Section 34 of Registration Act and description of the
property.

4. GSWS (SR) through CCA dashboard shall open the PRIMME ID compare the data
entered in PRIMME with the data provided in the document, and edit the data making
use of the edit button provided in the PRIMME report and after satisfaction as to the
correctness of the data in PRIMME ID shall generate a report of the data entered
through PRIMME and given it to the parties concerned for checking.

5. After obtaining the signatures of the parties in token of acceptance of correctness
of data shall click the accept button provided in the PRIMME report, where after CS
No.is generated in CCA.

6. The checkslip shall be opened and Photos and thumb impressions of the parties
Concerned will be captured. Cash receipt will be generated duly consuming the CFMS
challans through defacement module and proceed with the registration process in
CCA. Further the manual thumb impressions of the parties concerned shall also be
obtained in the thumb impression Register.

7. After capturing the photographs and thumb impression of the parties and witnesses
on electronic biometric devices including EKYC, a reverse endorsement shall be
generated on the document.

   

 
&  ° .»”»”»;x=x}LEE 7

8. The Sub Registrar (GS&WS) shall send the document to the concerned SR
electronically to check the Market value/Prohibited nature of property/Classification
of the property/nature of the document/Chargeability and other requirements under
Registration Act, 1908 for approval, after generation of pending document number.

 

9. The concerned Sub Registrar (R&S) shall check the document received from the
Sub Registrar (GS&WS) with reference to Market value/Prohibited property Register/
Classification of the property/nature of the document/Chargeability and other
requirements under Registration Act, within 1 hour.

10. On receipt of intimation from the concerned Sub Registrar: (R&S), the Panchayath
Secretary of V&W Secretariat shall assign regular number (the regular number will
be provided by the Jurisdictional SR in the dashboard of VSWS SR) to the document
and affix a certificate of registration, scan the document and return the document to
the parties on the same day.

11. Before the end of the day, all the scanned documents shall be digitally signed by the
Panchayath Secretary of concerned village secretariat.

12. The Details of the Document Registered has to be entered in the Account -A Register
as per the prescribed format given.

13. On completion of registration process the Sub-Registrar VS&WS shall initiate the
auto mutation process as prescribed by the government:

Secretary of VS & WS.

Sub Registrar (VSWS) shall guide the registering public to approach Survey/Revenue
department if the property requires sub-division prior to registration as per the
guidelines issued by the Government

for verification of data entered.

Kind attention is invited to the subject cited. The CCA Application Technicalities for
delivery of Registration services are mentioned below:

1) CCA Application work with VPN environment. It will not work with other private
networks Hence the VPN IP addresses should be assigned as mentioned below.

 
& .° .».”»”»+x,xLE 7

2) With respect to browser CCA Application by default will work with Slimjet browser,
if we use chrome browser it will work with Version 18.

 

3) Printers are available in all VSWS offices and No separate printer is required.

4) Flatbed scanners (Multifunction Printer cum Scanner) are available in all VSWS
offices as there is no requirement of duplex scanner at this point of time.

5)  EKYC Thumb impression devices (Mantra/Precision) are available in all VSWS offices.
6) Webcams are available in all VSWS offices for capturing the photos.

7) Thumb impression devices for the purpose of thumb capturing will be provided in two
days by VSWS department.

8) Account A and Thumb Impression Registers are supplied at the time of OTS
registrations.

9) VPN &CCA Login Information for VSWS Project.
a.) From Router to CPU LAN PORT via WIRELINE:

i. Login to Broadband router and assign SRO Broad Band IP to router in LAN
Setup.

ii. Assign subnet mask as 255.255.255.0 and disable the DHCP.

iii. Open Network Properties page and assign SRO Broadband IP as Gateway
and subnet with 255.255.255.0 and DNS 10.252.112.41, Alternative DNS is
4.2.2.2

iv. Connect the VPN login with SRO Credentials.
v. Check CCA with SRO employee login
b.) From Router to WIFI:

i. Login to Broadband router; assign SRO Broad Band IP to router and subnet
mask as 255.255.255.0 and disable the DHCP.

ii. Connect the LAN cable from router to any other device (printer/computer)
LAN port in order to enable Ethernet LAN.

iii. Open Network Properties and assign SRO Broadband IP as Gateway and
subnet with 255.255.255.0 and disable the Metric.

iv. Right Click on Wi-Fi (TCP/IPv4) Properties, and select Advanced Tab and
untick the Automatic metric box and change the metric value to 1.

v. Connect the VPN login as SRO Credentials.

vi. Right Click on VPN (TCP/IPv4) Properties, and select Advanced Tab and
untick the Automatic metric box and change the metric value to 10.

vii. Check CCA with SRO employee login.

2

   
tt

C) From Mobile HOTSPOT:

i. Connect the LAN cable from router to any other device (printer/computer)
LAN port in order to enable Ethernet LAN.

ii. Open Network Properties and assign gateway IP with SRO Broad Band IP and
assign Subnet with 255.255.255.224and select Advanced Tab and uncheck
the Automatic metric box and change the metric value to 1.

i. Right Click on Wi-Fi (TCP/IPv4) Properties, and select Advanced Tab and
untick the Automatic metric box and change the metric value to 3.

iv. Connect the VPN login as SRO Credentials.

v. Right Click on VPN (TCP/IPv4) Properties, and select Advanced Tab and untick
the Automatic metric box and change the metric value to 10.

vi. Check CCA with SRO employee login.

GOVERNMENT OF ANDHRA PRADESH
ABSTRACT

Registration and Stamps Department - Formation of New Sub-District under Sec.5
of Registration Act, 1908 to ensure speedy delivery of services at the door steps of the
people on completion of Resurvey - Notification Orders - Issued.

REVENUE (REG-1) DEPARTMENT
G.0.MS.No.284 Dated:23.06.2023

Read the following:

1. From the Commissioner and Inspector General of Registration and Stamps, A.P.
Vijayawada Lr.No. G1/1727110/2022, dated 18.06.2023

ORDER:

The Government of Andhra Pradesh is committed to welfare of its citizens and
has undertaken number of steps to bring in qualitative improvement in the service
delivery to the people of Andhra Pradesh. Further the Government is committed
to implement and decentralize governance through a transparent and effective
mechanism for public service delivery with an aim to improve living standards of the
people. The Government have taken a number of steps in this direction to fulfill the
avowed objectives of provision of various Government services at the door steps of
citizens and enable convergence of all line Departments to achieve the said objective.
As a step in this direction for proper implementation and institutionalization of the
concept to NAVARATHNALU as ore theme of governance. To achieve this objective, the

  
 

Government has established Village and Ward Secretariats in the State with the prime
objective of providing a strong governance for implementation of NAVARATHNALU,
accomplishing transparency and accountability in delivery of Government services
to the citizens and by ensuring convergence amongst various departments providing
services at the village level. Registration of documents is one of the important
activities of the Government and its decentralization would go a long way in ensuing
speedy and hassle-free services directly to the Citizens of the State at their door
step. This would help in

+ Corruption free governance

+ Easy access to public services

* Transparency and Accountability

* Qualitative service and speedy public delivery and
+ Easy verifiability of land records

2. The Government of Andhra Pradesh has taken up Re-survey as one of the flagship
program under "Suparipalana" and decided to conduct the Re- survey of entire State.
After re-survey decentralization of Registration services would go a long way in
fulfillment of the objective of the Government. Accordingly, vide reference 1st read
above, the Commissioner and Inspector General of Registration and Stamps, A-P.,
Vijayawada has submitted a proposal to the Government to form new sub-districts
of registration at village level. Therefore, it is decided to form new Sub-Districts at
Village / Ward secretariats for providing registration services at respective Village /
Ward Secretariats in the state under Phase Ill as a first step of convergence in the
scheme of Village / Ward Secretariats.

3. The Government after careful examination of the matter, hereby accept the proposals
of the Commissioner and Inspector General of Registration and Stamps, A-P.,
Vijayawada for formation of new Sub-Districts under Section 5 of the Registration Act,
1908 by declaring the jurisdiction of the present Sub-Districts with the jurisdiction
mentioned in the Annexure.

4. Accordingly, the following notification shall be published in the Extra-Ordinary Gazette
of the Andhra Pradesh, Dated:24.06.2023.

NOTIFICATION

In exercise of the powers conferred under Section 5 of the Registration Act, 1908 (Act.
No.16 of 1908) and in partial modification of all other notifications issued in this behalf, the
Governor of Andhra Pradesh hereby declare the jurisdiction of the existing Sub-districts
mentioned in column (4) of the Annexure with the Revenue Villages mentioned in column
(5) and form a new sub-district as mentioned in column (6) thereof with jurisdiction over
the villages mentioned in Column (5) and an office of Joint Sub-Registrar be established
at the place mentioned in Column(6) of the Annexure attached herewith.

 
   

& °°. »”»”»«+x%x}LEZ 7

1. The above notification shall come into force with immediate effect.

2. The Commissioner, Panchayath Raj & Rural Development Department, the
Director Gram Volunteers / Ward Volunteers & Village Secretaries / Ward
Secretaries Department, the Commissioner & Inspector General, Registration
and Stamps, A.P. Vijayawada and the District Collectors in the state shall take
further necessary action accordingly.

(BY ORDER AND IN THE NAME OF THE GOVERNOR OF ANDHRA PRADESH)

Dr. K.S.JAWAHAR REDDY
CHIEF SECRETARY TO GOVERNMENT

ORDER:

The Government of Andhra Pradesh is committed to welfare of its citizens and has
undertaken number of steps to bring in qualitative improvement in the service
delivery to the people of Andhra Pradesh. Further the Government is committed
to implement and decentralize governance through a transparent and effective
mechanism for public service delivery with an aim to improve living standards
of the people. The Government have taken a number of steps in this direction to
fulfill the avowed objectives of provision of various Government services at the
door steps of citizens and enable convergence of all line Departments to achieve

  
 

the said objective to achieve this objective, the Government has established Village
and Ward Secretariats in the State with the prime objective of providing a strong
governance for implementation of NAVARATHNALU, accomplishing transparency and
accountability in delivery of Government services to the citizens and by ensuring
convergence amongst various Departments providing services at the village level.
Registrations of documents is one of the important activities of the Government and
its decentralization would go a long way in ensuing speedy and hassle-free services
directly to the Citizens of the State at their door step. This would help in

* Corruption free governance

+ Easy access to public services

* Transparency and Accountability

* Qualitative service and speedy public delivery and
+ Easy verifiability of land records

The Government of Andhra Pradesh has taken up Re-survey as one of the flagship
program under "Suparipalana" and decided to conduct the Re- survey of entire
State. After re-survey decentralization of Registration services would go a long way
in fulfillment of the objective of the Government. Accordingly, vide reference 1st

Vijayawada had sent a proposal to the Government to establish the Registration
Offices at Village/Ward Secretariat level. Therefore, it is decided to establish

step of convergence of services at Village Secretariat/Ward Secretariat level.

The Government after careful examination of the matter, hereby accept the
proposals of the Commissioner and Inspector General of Registration and Stamps,
A.P., Vijayawada, in terms of Section 7(1) of the Registration Act, 1908 to declare the
Village/Ward Secretariats mentioned as in Annexure as the Office of Sub- Registrar

under Registration Act, 1908 with the jurisdiction mentioned in the Annexure.

Secretary to Government, Gram Volunteers / Ward Volunteers & Village Secretaries
/ Ward Secretaries Department and all the District Collectors in the state are
requested to provide necessary infrastructure including building, space for record
room and furniture, computer systems, scanner, printer, webcam, bio metric
devices, networking etc., for making the registration office being set up in Village/
Ward Secretariats in the State functional.

Accordingly, the following notification shall be published in the Extra- Ordinary
Gazette of the Andhra Pradesh, Dated:24.06.2023.

GOVERNMENT OF ANDHRA PRADESH
ABSTRACT

Registration and Stamps Department - To ensure speedy delivery of services at
the door steps of the people on completion of Re-survey- Appointment of Panchayat
Secretaries of Village Secretariats in the State as Joint Sub-Registrars to perform duties
of Joint Sub-Registrar under Sec.6 of Registration Act, 1908 - Orders Issued.

REVENUE(REG-1)DEPARTMENT
G.0.Ms.No.286 Dated:23.06.2023

Read the following:

1. From the Commissioner and Inspector General of Registration and Stamps, A.P.
Vijayawada Lr.No. G1/1727110/2022, dated 18.06.2023

2. G.0. Ms. No.285, Revenue (REGN.1) Dept dated. 23.06.2023.

2

   
   

ORDER:

Government through the concept of NAVARATNALU as core theme of Governance
proposed to provide hassle free service delivery to the public at Village/Ward level.
It was also decided to deliver Registration services at Village level. A Village/Ward
Secretariat system was created to provide various Governmental services at door
steps of the citizens and to enable convergence of all line departments to achieve the
said objective.

2 In pursuance of this policy Government had entrusted the public service delivery
to functionaries at the Village/Ward Secretariat level and issued service rules to
various functionaries and classified them as supervisory post, Health and Sanitary
post, Technical post and Miscellaneous post.

3. The Commissioner and Inspector General of Registration and Stamps of A.P.,
Vijayawada reported that among the various functionaries at the Village Secretariats
level, Panchayat Secretary shall be a graduate and his job chart indicates that it is a
supervisory post and delivers multiple services at the village level. Whereas all other
functionaries at the Village Secretariat level are either technical or specialized posts
in nature. Registration of the documents is one of the functions of the Panchayat
Secretaries as per the job chart issued vide reference 2nd cited.

4. The Commissioner and Inspector General of Registration and Stamps of A.P.,
Vijayawada further reported that in pursuance to the Government Lr.No.REV08-
22057/32/2021-General-IGRS, dt.07.06.2022, an extensive training was imparted to
the Panchayat Secretaries of Village Secretariats both in theory and practical modules
for a period of 180/60 days respectively. An assessment test was also conducted to
these functionaries. The Commissioner and Inspector General of Registration and
Stamps of A.P., Vijayawada further reported that Panchayath Secretaries are fit to
handle registration services relative to the properties at the Village Secretariat level.
It is also reported that a detailed Standard Operative Procedure was also drawn and
proposed to send the documents presented for registration before the Panchayat
Secretary/Ward Administrative Secretary to the designated regular Sub-Registrar
concerned for guidance.

5. Inthe reference cited 2nd read above, orders have been issued declaring the office of
Village/Ward Secretariats as mentioned in the Annexure as Office of Sub- Registrar/
Joint Sub-Registrars under section 7(1) of the Registration Act, 1908 for registration
of documents relating to properties in the said villages, under phase- Ill

6. Government, after careful examination of the issue hereby accept the proposals of the
Commissioner and Inspector General of Registration and Stamps of A.P., Vijayawada
to appoint Panchayat Secretaries/Ward Administrative Secretaries as mentioned in
the Annexure as Joint Sub-Registrars to perform the duties of Joint Sub-Registrars

167

 
 

&  ° ».».”»”»+x=x}FEE 7

for registration of documents relating to the properties of the said Villages/Wards.
The Digital Assistant of the concerned Village secretariat shall assist the Panchayat
Secretary to perform the duties of Joint Sub-Registrar.

 

7. Accordingly, the following notification shall be published in the Extra- Ordinary
Gazette of the Andhra Pradesh, Dated:24.06.2023.

NOTIFICATION

In exercise of the powers conferred under Section 6 of the Registration Act, 1908 (Act
No.16 of 1908) and in partial modification of all other notifications issued in this behalf,
the Governor of Andhra Pradesh hereby appoints the Panchayath Secretaries/Ward
Administrative Secretaries in Village/Ward Secretariats as mentioned in the Annexure
as Joint Sub-Registrars to perform the duties of Joint Sub- Registrar for registration
of documents relating to the properties in the Villages/Wards, under their territorial
jurisdiction.

1. The above notification shall come into force with immediate effect.

2. The Digital Assistant of the concerned Village Secretariat shall assist the
Panchayat Secretary to perform the duties of the Joint Sub-Registrar.

3. The Panchayat Raj & Rural Development Department, Gram Volunteers/
Ward Volunteers & Village Secretaries/Ward Secretaries Department and the
Commissioner and Inspector General, Registration and Stamps, A.P., Viiayawada
and all the Collectors & District Magistrates in the state shall take further
necessary action accordingly.

GOVERNMENT OF ANDHRA PRADESH
ABSTRACT

GAD - Employees Welfare - Conduct of Employee Grievance Day on every 3" Friday of
the month by the District Collectors & Heads of Departments on the individual grievances
of employees - Orders - Issued.

GENERAL ADMINISTRATION (CABINET-II) DEPARTMENT

G.0.Rt.No.1233 Dated:24.06.2023.
OK

2

   
i

ORDE!

 

The Government is committed to the welfare of its employees and seeks to promote
work like balance to enable the employees and their families to lead happy lives.
To achieve this and for the welfare of employees, the Government gives priority
to attend their individual Grievances. Therefore, for paying proper attention by the
concerned authorities in attending the individual grievances of the employees on
priority basis and to resolve them in a time bound manner, it is considered to conduct
Employees Grievance Day every month.

2. Accordingly, Government hereby order to conduct Employee Grievance Day, on every
third Friday of the month by all the District Collectors and Heads of Department in
the State for attending the grievance of employees and resolving the issues in a time
bound manner, as prescribed below:

i. | Employees can submit any type of Individual Grievances to the District Collectors/
HoDs including personal Grievances.

ii. All grievances shall be registered with unique IDs in the JKC (Jaganannaku
Chebudham) portal.

iii. There shall be a facility to track the status of the Grievances by using unique IDs.

iv. The Secretaries /HoDs shall fix the timelines for redressal of all types of
Grievances.

v.  Collectors/ HoDs shall invariably review the ATRS of the Grievances so received
bi-weekly.

3. The Information Technology, Electronic & Communications Department shall take
necessary action to make provision in the JKC Portal for registering each individual
grievance of the employee with Unique ID and facilitate to track the status of the
Grievance by using unique ID/Phone Number.

4. All Spl.Chief Secretary/Prl.Secretary/Secretary to Govt., Departments of Secretariat,
Heads of Departments and District Collectors shall take necessary further action in
the matter accordingly.

(BY ORDER AND IN THE NAME OF THE GOVERNOR OF ANDHRA PRADESH)

Dr. K.S. JAWAHAR REDDY
CHIEF SECRETARY TO GOVERNMENT

No: 42/15/2022-PAPWID
GOVERNMENT OF INDIA
MINISTRY OF PERSONNEL, PGA PENSIONS
DEPARTMENT OF PENSION & PENSIONERS' WELFARE

3 Floor, Lok Nayak Bhawan
Khan Market, New Delhi-110003
Date-25 Oct, 2022

OFFICE MEMORANDUM

Sub: — Clarification regarding whether deductions towards commutation are required
to be made from family pension for the remaining period in cases where the
pensioner dies before the restoration of commuted pension-Reg.

OK

In accordance with Rule 5 of CCS (Commutation of Pension) Rules, 1981, a Government
servant can commute for a lump-sum payment of an amount not exceeding 40 per
cent of his pension Further, in accordance with Rule 10-A of CCS (Commutation
of Pension) Rules, 1981 the commuted amount of the pension shall be restored
on completion of fifteen years from the date of reduction of pension on account of
commutation becomes operative in accordance with Rule 6.

2. References/representations have been received in this Department seeking
clarification whether deduction towards commutation are required to be made from
family pension for the remaining period in cases where the pensioner dies before the
restoration of commuted pension

3. It is clarified that in such cases monthly commuted amount of pension is not required
to be deducted from family pension and family pension shall be paid in full without
any deduction in this regard.

(Charanjit Taneja)
Under Secretary

represented that many of the employees of AP Secretariat and HODS etc., have not
shifted their families in view of the studies of their children in professional courses/
occupations at Hyderabad. Most of the employees are now able to go to Hyderabad
on every Friday using "Five day week facility" provided by the Government. He has,
therefore requested the Government to extend the "Five Day Week facility" for a
further period of one year w.e.f 27.06.2023.

Government Organizations having this facility earlier and functioning in Amaravati
capital region area with the working hours from 10.00 AM to 05.30 PM for a further
period of one year with effect from 27.06.2023.

GOVERNMENT OF ANDHRA PRADESH
ABSTRACT

Appointment of 12th Pay Revision Commission - Orders - Issued.
GENERAL ADMINISTRATION (SCA) DEPARTMENT
GO.Ms.No. 68 Dated: 12-07-2023

Read:-

From the Finance (PC.I) Department e-file bearing No. FINO1- HROPCTA/33/2023-
PC-TA, dated: 30.06.2023.

ORDER:

The Pay Scales of the State Government employees were revised with effect from
01.07.2018 with monetary benefit from 01.04.2020.

2. Inthe reference 1st read above, the Finance Department have submitted proposals for
constitution of 12th Pay Revision Commission for the State Government employees.
Government after careful consideration, has decided to constitute the 12th Pay
Revision Commission for the State Government employees.

3. Accordingly, Government hereby constitute the 12th Pay Revision Commission
and hereby appoint Dr. Manmohan Singh, IAS (1985) (Retired) as Pay Revision
Commissioner. The terms and conditions of appointment of Pay Revision Commissioner
will be issued separately.

4. The terms of reference of the Commission shall be as follows:

i. To evolve the principles which may govern the structure of emoluments and the
conditions of service of various categories of employees of the State Government,
Local Bodies and Aided Institutions, Non-teaching staff of the Universities Including
Acharya N.G. Ranga Agricultural University, Jawaharlal Nehru Technological
University, Work charged employees and full-time contingent employees, which
have a financial bearing, taking into account the total packet of benefits available
to them and suggest changes therein which may be desirable and feasible;

ay)

 
 

vi.

vii.

viii.

 

(a) The Commission however shall, not deal with the teaching staff in
Government Colleges and Government Aided Private Colleges drawing UGC/
AICTE and ICAR scales. The Commission shall also not deal with the officers
of A.P. State Higher Judicial Service and A.P. State Judicial Service who are
drawing Pay Scales as recommended by the Second National Judicial Pay
Commission.

To examine as to what extent the existing DA may be merged in pay and, to
evolve consequent new set of pay scales merging DA therein and to suggest the
mode of fixation of pay in the Revised Pay Scales.

To study the Automatic Advancement Scheme as modified from time to time
keeping in view the anomalies that have arisen during the Implementation of the
said scheme and also to examine whether the said scheme should continue in
its present form and to make the recommendations in this regard.

To examine the need for various Special Pays, Compensatory and various other
Allowances and other perquisites in cash or kind now allowed and to make
recommendations regarding their continuance or otherwise and if continuance
is recommended, the modifications, if any are deemed, desirable with regard to
their rates, terms and other conditions which should govern them in future.

To examine and review the existing pension structure for pensioners, and make
recommendations which may be desirable and feasible.

To evolve principles / norms/ criteria / guidelines for making any category of
post as gazetted and give suggestions in respect of the categories of posts
proposed either by Associations / administrative departments for making certain
categories of posts as gazetted.

Review of the existing human resources of all departments in tune with the
contemporary requirements including contract/ outsourcing personnel
particularly in the context of State bifurcation.

To give its recommendations on any other matter referred to it by the State
Government during the tenure of its office.

In formulating its recommendations, the Commission may take into account the
overall financial position of the State.

The Pay Revision Commission may lay down its own procedure for receiving
representations, examining witnesses and calling for information. The Departments
of Secretariat and Heads of Departments shall furnish all necessary information as
and when required by the Pay Revision Commission and extend such co-operation
and assistance as may be required by him.

The Pay Revision Commission may ordinarily function from Velagapudi, but may tour
within or outside, the State, if necessary.

7. The Pay Revision Commission will submit its report to the Government within a period
of one year from the date of assumption of charge of the Pay Revision commissioner.

CIRCULAR NO: CARD/e-1849213/2023, D ated: 14/07/2023
CIRCULAR MEMO
Sub: R&S, Dept. - CCA - CARD 2.0 - Deployment of New version of CCA le CARD 2.0-
CARD PRIMME -Testing and Implementation in the SROS in the state - Reg.
KK

The attention is invited to the subject cited. A New version of CCA i.e CARD PRIMME
application is developed and is ready for UAT. The CARD PRIMME application is developed
to address the current challenges in document generation, payment of Duties and slot
booking in the application as well as infrastructure and to provide speedy and better
services to the registering public of Andhra Pradesh.

In this regard, as part of pilot project four Registration districts have been identified
i.e., Visakhapatnam, Vijayawada, Guntur and Tirupati for testing purpose in their respective
R.Os.

There are two ways of Registration process

1.) Data entry and document uploading. This is the case where citizen prepares his
document without using document generation module provided by the department.

Three modes of testing has to be done in this case.

A. Data Entry using CARD PRIMME of user side application and switch to CARD
PRIMME of SRO side for verification and complete registration process.

B. Data Entry and verification using CARD 2.0 application.
C. Data Entry though POST Manual method in CARD 2.0 application.

176

  
—_—

2.) Data entry With Document Generation. This is the case where citizen prepares his
document using document generation module provided by the department.

Here testing has to be done.

A. Use CARD PRIMME for document generation and verification using CARD PRIMME
SRO side.

Therefor the aforementioned DIGS and DRS ensure the implementation of pilot CARD
PRIMME is carried out smoothly and provide feedback from time to time basis and the
remaining Deputy Inspector Generals and the District Registrars conduct random testing
of CARD PRIMME and provide feedback to this office.

This testing process will continue up 21.07.2023 and thereafter it is made live for all
the SROS in the State

The CARD PRIMME URL is: http://cardprimme.rs.ap.gov.in/PDE
The CARD 2.0 URL is: http://10.96.47.93:5005/

V Rama Krishna
Commissioner and Inspector General of
Registration and Stamps, A.P., Vilayawada.

GOVERNMENT OF ANDHRA PRADESH
ABSTRACT

Registration & Stamps Department - Levy of Registration fee on document of
Paripassu nature Under section (78) of Registration Act, 1908 Modified Orders Issued.

REVENUE (REGN.I) DEPARTMENT
G.0.Ms.No.347 Dated:24.07.2023.
Read the following:-
1. G.0.Ms.No.463, Revenue (Regn-1) Department, dated. 17-08-2013

2. From the Commissioner & 1,.G. of Registration and Stamps, AP, Letter No. G1/
Rev08-22027/123/2022, dt. 23-6-2022 & 17-8-2022.

ORDER:

1. Inthe reference 1st read above, Government have issued orders prescribing the rates
of Registration fee for Registration of documents and other services under Section 78
of the Registration Act, 1908. In the said GO at serial number (14) under Category (1)

rs

 
 

of the Table of Fee, Registration fee 60.5% was prescribed in respect of instruments
evidencing creation of Paripassu Charge.

In the reference 2nd read above, the Commissioner and Inspector General of
Registration and Stamps, Andhra Pradesh has reported that the deeds evidencing
Paripassu Charge which are consequential documents to the aforesaid Deposit of
Title Deeds or Mortgages are prescribed with a fee of © 0.5% without any ceiling
limit. In general, a deed evidencing Paripassu Charge covers many Mortgages/
Deposit of Title Deeds with corresponding loan amounts. As such the aggregate
amount of the said Paripassu charge will usually be very high and runs into crores of
rupees. The present levy of registration fee @ 0.5% without any ceiling limit on the
deeds of Paripassu Charge is causing hard ship to the entrepreneurs seeking loans
from various Banks and Financial Institutions as the loan amounts are often runs into
hundreds of crores.

The Commissioner and Inspector General (Registration and Stamps), Andhra Pradesh
has therefore requested the Government to revise the above fee for the instruments
evidencing Paripassu Charge and to levy the fee 0.5% subject to a maximum of Rs.
5,00,000/- (Rupees Five lakhs only).

Government after careful examination of the matter, have decided to revise the
registration fee for the instruments evidencing Paripassu: Charge and hereby levy
the fee © 0.5% subject to a maximum of Rs. 5,00,000/- (Rupees Five Lakhs only)
subject to the following conditions:-

a. The Commissioner and Inspector General, Registration and Stamps, Andhra
Pradesh shall take appropriate steps for ensuring that the proposed cap is
applicable only for registration of Paripassu documents, so that no income
leakage occurs on account of inadvertently reducing fees on other documents;

b. The above revision of the fee shall be valid for a period of (12) months only from
the date of the issue of the order and the further continuation will be reviewed.

The following notification shall be published in an Extraordinary Issue of the Andhra
Pradesh Gazette, dated 24.07.2023.

NOTIFICATION

In exercise of the powers conferred under Section 78 of the Registration Act, 1908
(Act XVI of 1908), the Governor of Andhra Pradesh hereby makes the following
amendment to the "Table of Fee" under the Registration Act, 1908 issued in G.0.Ms.
No.463, Revenue (Registration-1) Department, dated the 17 August, 2013 and
published at pages 1 to 3 of Part 1 of Extraordinary issue of the Andhra Pradesh
Gazette, dated the 26th August, 2013 as subsequently amended from time to time.

178

 

 

 
LC

AMENDMENT

In the said "Table of Fee", for the entry relating to Serial Number (14) under Category
(1), the following shall be substituted namely:-

THE ANDHRA PRADESH GAZETTE
PART IV-B EXTRAORDINARY
PUBLISHED BY AUTHORITY

ANDHRA PRADESH ACTS, ORDINANCES AND REGULATIONS Etc.,
The following is the authoritative text in English Language of the Ordinance
promulgated by the Governor on the 27 July, 2023 is being published under Article 348(3)
of the Constitution of India for general information:-

ANDHRA PRADESH ORDINANCE No. 9 OF 2023.

179

 
&  °» .»”»”»«x,x}FE

AN ORDINANCE FURTHER TO AMEND THE ANDHRA PRADESH ASSIGNED LANDS
(PROHIBITION OF TRANSFERS) ACT 1977.

Whereas, the Andhra Pradesh Assigned Lands (Prohibition of Transfers) Act, 1977
is a protective legislation and it prohibits the transfer of lands assigned to landless poor
persons for the purpose of cultivation and provides for restoration of such transferred
lands to the original assignee and also for punishment to the persons acquiring such
lands;

 

And whereas, the condition of non-alienability of assigned lands impacted the
poorest of the poor by preventing them from making use of the same for emergent family
needs;

And whereas, proposals were received for granting transfer rights to the assignees
of their assigned lands after elapse of certain period from the date of assignment,

And whereas Government constituted a committee under the Chairmanship of the
Hon ble Minister for Revenue, Registration & Stamp vide GORI No 822 Revenue (Lands)
Dept dt 30 09.2022 to examine the issue of right to set the assigned lands for the welfare
and financial ease of the assignees

And whereas, the committee after examining the relevant revenue laws in force
in the neighbouring states of Tamilnadu, Karnataka and Kerala and the realistic ground
situation in the state inter alia, recommended that the original assignee or his/her legal
heir may be permitted to alienate the assigned land after a period of Twenty years from
the date of assignment as per the procedure prescribed and without reference to the
Government and in case, the adjoining land is earmarked for any use other than agricultural
use in master plan, the assignee shall be paid on par with the current basic value of the
land by the purchaser of such assigned land,

And whereas, the Government has accepted the above recommendations of the
committee and decided to amend the above Act suitably.

Whereas, the Legislature of the State is not now in session and the Governor of
Andhra Pradesh is satisfied that the circumstances exist which render it necessary for
him to take immediate action.

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by clause (1) of article 213 of
the Constitution of India, the Governor hereby promulgates the following Ordinance-

SHORT TITLE AND COMMENCEMENT

1. (1) This Ordinance may be called the Andhra Pradesh Assigned Lands (Prohibition of
Transfers) (Amendment) Ordinance, 2023.

(2) It extends to the whole of the State of Andhra Pradesh,

(3) It shall come into force at once.

  
i —

AMENDMENT OF SECTION 2 ACT NO 9 OF 1977

2 Inthe Andhra Pradesh Assigned Lands (Prohibition of Transfers) Act, 1977. (hereinafter
referred to as the principal Act), in Section 2, in Clause (1) after the words, ‘subject to
the condition of non-alienation’ the words within a period of twenty (20) years from
the date of assignment in the case of lands assigned for agricultural purpose, and
within a period of ten (10) years from the date of assignment in case of house sites"
shall be inserted.

AMENDMENT OF SECTION 3 ACT

3 In the Principal Act, in Section 3, for sub-section (2), the following sub-section shall
be substituted, namely.

(2) (i) No landless poor person or his/her legal heir, shall transfer any land assigned
for agricultural purpose, and no person shall acquire any such assigned land,
either by purchase, gift, lease (except in the case of Lease to the Andhra Pradesh
Green Energy Corporation Ltd, for use as deemed fit and including for usage of
non-agriculture purpose)" mortgage. exchange or otherwise, within a period of
twenty (20) years from the date of assignment

(ii) After expiry of the above period of twenty years, such assigned land may be
alienated by the assignee or his/her legal heirs as per the procedure prescribed,
without further reference to the Government.

(iii) where any land adjoining such assigned land is earmarked for any use other than
agriculture use in the master plan of the area, the assignee or his/her legal heirs
shall be paid current basic value of said adjoining non-agricultural land by the
purchaser of the such assigned land.".

S. ABDUL NAZEER,
Governor of Andhra Pradesh.

G. SATYA PRABHAKARA RAO,
Secretary to Government,
Legal and Legislative Affairs & Justice,
Law Department.

The attention of the DIGS, DRS and Registering Officers in the State is invited to the
subject and references cited. Through reference 7th and 8th cited certain instructions
were issued regarding, procedure to be taken while registering agriculture and Non
agriculture properties..

Through the Circular Memo 7 cited, certain instructions were issued at Sl.No 3,4,5
of Part A, the procedure to be followed for registration of documents basing on LPM
Numbers and sub division of such properties. In order to follow the said Instructions, it
was further clarified that the above instructions shall be followed after the web land is
updated with LPM numbers. Therefore, the registering officers shall ensure to obtain both
LPM numbers and its corresponding Survey numbers in the document and also capture
the same while entering the data without fail.

In the said circular, it was mentioned that these instructions shall be applicable to
the urban local bodies only. In order to avoid confusion for the field staff, it is clarified
that these instructions are applicable to the urban local bodies which means Municipal
corporations, municipalities and Nagara Panchayaths, and they are not applicable to the
other areas falling under Urban development authority or any panchayath areas.

HEH HEH,B VSO HWSOOWS SHSO Joowsswsw, vow H:50

GOVERNMENT OF ANDHRA PRADESH
ABSTRACT

General Administration Department- The Andhra Pradesh State and Subordinate
Service Rules, 1996 - Relinquishment of Rights by Members of Service - Amendment to
Rule 28 of the Andhra Pradesh State and Subordinate Service Rules, 1996 - Notification-
Orders - Issued.

GENERAL ADMINISTRATION (SERVICES-D) DEPARTMENT

G.0.Ms.No.92. Dated 28.08.2023.
Read the following:
1. G.0.Ms.No.436, G.A. (Ser.D) Dept., dated 15.10.1996.

2. Orders of Hon'ble High Court of Andhra Pradesh in WP No.21718 of 2022 dated
24.11.2022 & dated 05.07.2023.

3. Orders of Hon'ble High Court of Andhra Pradesh in WP No.21718 of 2022 dated
02.08.2023.

OK

  
   

 

ORDE!

The Andhra Pradesh State and Subordinate Service Rules, 1996 issued in the G.0.
first read above, defined relinquishment of rights by members in Rule 28 as follows:

“any member of a service may, in writing, relinquish any right or privilege to which
he may be entitled to, under these rules or the special rules, if, in the opinion of
the appointing authority such relinquishment is not opposed to public interest. Such
relinquishment once made will be final and irrevocable. Nothing contained in these
rules or the special rules shall be deemed to require the recognition of any right or
privilege to the extent to which it has been so relinquished:

Provided that no conditional relinquishment or relinquishment of right for a temporary
period shall be permitted”.

2. Inthe reference 2nd read above, the Hon'ble High Court of Andhra Pradesh has been
pleased to issue the following order, dated 24.11.2022 in WP No.21718 of 2022
filed by Smt J.Radha, Lecturer in Social Science, Government General Hospital,
Ananthapuramu District:

“This Court is constrained to observe that the "gross perversity" as termed by this
Court could have been avoided by the Official Respondents herein if they would
have suitably amended the Rule 28 of the Andhra Pradesh State and Subordinate
Service Rules, 1996 in terms of the interpretation given by a Division Bench of this
Hon'ble Court way back in the year 2006 (which has become final in all respects).
The Respondents are directed to suitably amend Rule 28 of the Andhra Pradesh State
and Subordinate Service Rules, 1996"

3. In the order dated 09.08.2006, the Hon'ble High Court of Andhra Pradesh in WP
No 26654 of 2005 filed by the District Education Officer, Kurnool and 3 others has
disposed of the case with the following directions:-

The Tribunal, in its earlier order in Smt. S. Swarna Kumari Vs. Dy. Commissioner of
Prohibition and Excise [1], considered the scope and implication of Rule 28 of the A.P.
State & Subordinate Services Rules and held thus:

careful perusal of Rule-28, extracted above, would reveal the following:

 

(1) An option is given to any member of a service to relinquish any right or privilege
to which he may be entitled to, under the relevant rules (this includes right or
privilege of promotion).

(2) Such relinquishment is subject to acceptance of the appointing authority.
Who must be satisfied before acting upon such relinquishment that such
relinquishment was not opposed to public interest.

(3) Such relinquishment once made will be final and irrevocable.

 
 

(4) Once such relinquishment has come into force, the authorities concerned shall
not be required to recognise any right or privilege to the extent to which it has
been so relinquished.

The provisotothis Rule contemplates that conditional relinquishment or relinquishment
of a right for a temporary period shall not be permitted....... Viewed in this context,
a relinquishment of a right or privilege to promotion would only mean that his right
to be considered for promotion, while he was occupying a particular place in the
seniority list, at a time when he was eligible for promotion by virtue of passing of the
tests etc., as required under the then existing rules and in respect of a vacancy, that
has arisen at the relevant time has been relinquished. This relinquishment may be
permanent.

It is true that conditional relinquishment is not permissible. What is means is
that in respect of that particular opportunity for promotion a member of a service
cannot relinquish with a rider that he would claim that privilege of promotion on
that happening of certain contingency. For example, if a member of a service has
relinquished his right or privilege of promotion on the ground of his sickness, he
cannot be allowed to claim that offers of promotion may be revived after he is cured
of his sickness.

Similarly, the provision that he cannot be permitted to relinquish temporarily would
mean that in respect of a particular opportunity for promotion, the employee cannot
be allowed to claim that the offer of promotion may be kept open for him for a
particular period.

The real question is what privilege or right has been relinquished?

This assumes significance in view of the language used in Rule-28 of the state and
Subordinate Service Rules which is to the following effect:

“Nothing contained in these rules or the special Rules shall be deemed to require the
recognition of any right or privilege to the extent to which it has been so relinquished."

This would indicate that the authorities concerned are not required to recognise any
right or privilege of a member of a service to the extent to which it as been so
relinquished. The words "to the extent to which it has been so relinquished" appears
significant.

So, the question, therefore, is where a member of a service, who had a privilege or a
right to be considered for promotion, at a particular stage, has relinquished his right,
the relinquishment has to be interpreted in the background of what has been stated
above that a privilege or right of promotion depends on concatenation of several
circumstances. The extent of relinquishment has to be determined in the context
of that particular occasion when the right or privilege for promotion had arisen for

 

 

 
 

a member of a service. The relinquishment of an opportunity for promotion, which
arose for an employee occupying certain place in seniority list in the year 2001,
in view of a vacancy that arose then, in view of the fact that she was eligible for
promotion in the light of the criteria laid down in the Rules, would mean that the
extent the privilege has been relinquished is confined to the privilege related to that
particular vacancy which was available to her by virtue of the above mentioned
circumstances. The Rule cannot be interpreted to mean that the relinquishment was
in respect of future vacancies also.

The learned Government Pleader seeks to contend that such an interpretation would
render the provision in the Rule that such relinquishment will be permanent nugatory.
We are unable to accept this contention.

As far as that particular vacancy is concerned, the employee's relinquishment is
final. He cannot claim later that he may be deemed to have been promoted to that
particular vacancy and that his seniority may be fixed as if he was promoted to that
vacancy. Accepting such interpretation would mean that if a member of service,
who has relinquished his promotion, at one stage, is promoted subsequently when
another vacancy arose, he will be junior to a person, who inspite of being junior to
this member, was promoted to the vacancy relinquished by him in the promotion
post.

Inthe light of the above discussion, we have no hesitation in holding that relinquishment
of right or privilege of promotion to a particular vacancy would amount to permanent
relinquishment of right of privilege for promotion to that particular vacancy. The
Rule- 28 of the State and Subordinate Service Rules cannot be read or interpreted to
mean that his right to be considered for promotion to any vacancy arising in future
also is permanently extinguished. Such an interpretation would lead to frustration
and unrest in the service defeating the object of promoting efficiency and harmonious
functioning......" (emphasis supplied)

We are in complete agreement with and record our approval of the interpretation
placed by the Tribunal, in 0.A.No. 715 of 2004 dated 23.03.2004, on Rule 28 of the
AP. State and Subordinate Service Rules.

In this context, it also necessary to take note of Rule 11(b) of the A.P. State and
Subordinate Rules which prescribes the time for a person appointed to a post,
otherwise than by direct recruitment, to join the said post. Rule 11(b) reads as under:

“(b) Time to join a post on appointment otherwise than by direct recruitment:- A
person appointed to a post, otherwise than by direct recruitment, shall be allowed
joining time of 15 (fifteen) days from the date of receipt of the order of appointment
sent to the candidate by Registered Post with acknowledgement due or by any other
means. If a person fails to join the post within the stipulated period of 15(fifteen)
days or evades to join the post by proceeding on leave, the offer of appointment

 
 

shall be treated as automatically cancelled and the name of the candidate shall be
deemed to have been omitted from the list of the approved candidates and she/he
shall forfeit his right of appointment both for the present and in future for the post."

Rule 11 (b), as amended by G.0.Ms.No.123 dated 14.03.2001 with effect from
19.12.2000, specifically provides that if a person fails to join the post within the
stipulated period of fifteen days or evades to join the post, the offer of appointment shall
be treated as automatically cancelled, the name of the candidate shall be deemed to
have been omitted from the list of approved candidates and that she/he shall forfeit
his right of appointment both for the present and in future for the post. Rule 11(b), as
it then was, provided that failure to join the promoted post within the stipulated time
would result in forfeiture of the person's right for appointment both for the present
and in the future. Unlike Rule 11(b), as inserted by G.0.Ms.No.123 dated 14.03.2001,
Rule 28, which was in existence even prior thereto, does not specifically provide
for forfeiture of future consideration for promotion. Relinquishment of promotion
in 1977 will only disentitle the respondent-applicant from claiming promotion to
the post which was filled up by way of promotion in the year 1977 and it is not
open for her thereafter to seek withdrawal of her relinquishment or to contend that
despite her relinquishment, she should have been considered for promotion to the
said post. Rule 28 does not prohibit consideration of the respondent-applicant's case
for promotion forever, for a right to be considered for promotion, if an employee is
otherwise eligible, is a fundamental right under Article 16(1) of the Constitution of
India. (Ajit Singh Il Vs. State of Punjab [2] and Delhi Jal Board Vs. Mahinder Singh
[3]). It is also well settled that fundamental rights cannot be waived (Olga Tellis Vs.
Bombay Municipal Corporation [4]).

While the respondent-applicant did not specifically waive her right to be considered
for promotion, no such waiver can be said to have been made by necessary implication
either. Rule 28 does not disentitle a member of a service from being considered
for promotion in a future vacancy merely because he/she had relinquished his/her
right under the Rules for promotion earlier. While the submission of the learned
Government Pleader that the Tribunal had not examined the scope and purport of
Rule 28 may indeed be true, on a reading of Rule 28, we do not find any reason to
take a view different from the one taken by the Tribunal. The jurisdiction of this Court
under Article 226 of the Constitution of India is supervisory and not appellate. We are
satisfied that the conclusion of the Tribunal and the relief granted by it is not contrary
to law. We see no reason, therefore, to exercise our discretionary jurisdiction under
Article 226 of the Constitution of India to interfere with the order of the Tribunal.

The writ petition fails and is accordingly dismissed. However, in the circumstances,
without costs.

In the order 3rd read above, the Hon'ble High Court of Andhra Pradesh is pleased to
direct the Principal Secretary of Department of Law and the Principal Secretary of
Department of General Administration to file affidavits as regards the ‘action taken’
in the matter.

 

 

 
SS

6.

Government after careful examination of the matter and in compliance with the
directions of the Hon'ble High Court of Andhra Pradesh have decided to amend Rule
28 of Andhra Pradesh State and Subordinate Service Rules, 1996 suitably Accordingly,
the following notification will be published in the Andhra Pradesh Gazette:

NOTIFICATION

In exercise of the powers conferred by the proviso to Article 309 of Constitution of
India and of all other powers hereunto enabling, the Governor of Andhra Pradesh
hereby makes the following amendment to the Andhra Pradesh State and Subordinate
Service Rules, 1996 issued in G.0.Ms.No.436, General Administration (Services-D)
Department, dated the 15th October, 1996 as subsequently amended from time to
time:

AMENDMENT
In the said rules, in Rule-28, after the proviso, the following shall be added namely:-
Explanation:

(i) Inrespect of promotion to amember of service, who has exercised relinquishment
of promotion due to any reason. Rule 28 does not disentitle him/her from being
considered for promotion in a future vacancy merely because he/she had
relinquished his/her right under the Rules for promotion earlier.

(ii) Relinquishment of right or privilege of promotion to a particular vacancy would
not amount to permanent relinquishment of right of privilege for promotion to
that particular vacancy. The Rule-28 cannot be read or interpreted to mean that
his/her right to be considered for promotion to any vacancy arising in future also
is permanently extinguished.

(iii) The relinquishment of an opportunity for promotion, which arose for an employee
occupying certain place in seniority list in the panel year, in view of a vacancy
that arose then, in view of the fact that he/she was eligible for promotion in
the light of the criteria laid down in the Rules, would mean that the extent
the privilege has been relinquished is confined to the privilege related to that
particular vacancy/panel year which was available to him/her by virtue of the
above mentioned circumstances. The Rule cannot be interpreted to mean that
the relinquishment was in respect of future vacancies also. Therefore, the right
of the Member of Service shall be considered in future vacancy for promotion, if
otherwise eligible.

A copy of this order is available in online and can be accessed at https://apegazette.
cgg.gov.in

(BY ORDER AND IN THE NAME OF THE GOVERNOR OF ANDHRA PRADESH)

Dr. K.S. JAWAHAR REDDY

application is developed and is ready for implementation. The CARD PRIMME application
is developed to address the current challenges in the present software like scalability
and adaptability to modern technologies. The present CARD PRIMME software is designed
to have a good citizen interface by separating the front activities of citizen from the
backend activities of the Department officials.The citizen interface is designed in a simple
Application form with a tracking service to submit his application to any Sub registrar
office of his choice or to the office of VSWS where the property is situated. The citizen
can submit the Application form through multiple channels (through Internet, Home,
VSWS, CSCs, and others or in person in any Sub Registrar office of his choice.) In order to
simplify the entire registration service the citizen is provided with a facility of document
generation online through the system. A choice is also given to the citizen for uploading
of a prepared document without using the document generation software at the time
of application form itself. After entering the property details citizen can get the market
value i.e. guidelines value of the property entered in a transparent way and duty charges
is displayed can pay the duties online. The citizen can book slot online of his choice to get
speedy and better services.

The citizen or his representative after submitting his application online visits the
office during where his slot time. At the verification counter of the Sub Registrar office the
application together with the documents will be retrieved from the centralised database.
The staff at verification counter checks all the details of application of the citizen right
from the prohibitory nature of the properties to the Market value of the property and
payment details and also the identity of the parties present for registration through E-
kyc. The system provides the checklist of standard reasons for the Sub Registrar to record
his observations for registration as well as for refusal and to the reasons for keeping the
document pending.

Sub Registrar can assign the roles of verification of different modules among his
staff members for better scrutiny and for reducing the dwell time of the citizen at Sub
Registrar office.

 

 

 
 

For the identification of parties and the signature of the parties for admission of
execution of the document Aadhaar enabled E-services are implemented and the reverse
endorsement of the document presented the Aadhaar photo of the parties are taken
with their Aadhaar enabled E-signature. If the parties upload the electronic document or
generates the document through the system (Rather than bringing the physical document
by public) a choice is given to the citizen to execute the document also through Aadhaar
enabled E-signatures and he need not bring the document physically and present the
document physically. In this case the document will be presented electronically and all
the process of execution and admission of execution is done electronically and there is
no need of scanning the document. The registration of the document can be completed
through E-signature and the recording of the document in the central server will be done
through the digital signature of the Sub Registrar. In this process the dwell time of the
citizen at the Sub Registrar office is drastically reduced.

The citizen side application submission as well as document uploading/generation
are going to be mandatory for all types of documents and a date will be announced.
Until then as a stop gap process the data entry can be done at Sub Registrar offices as
well as physical document submission will be followed with scanning module. But the
Aadhaar enabled identity, E-kyc and E-signature is mandatory for reverse endorsements.
Sub Registrar also has to E-sign the endorsements and no physical signature is allowed.

As the PRIMME stands for property registration integration and mutation made easy,
the citizen is given a new service of provisional mutation certificate of his property along
with the registered document in case of agricultural properties. The process of giving the
service of provisional mutation certificate for urban properties is in development and will
be released soon.

As the Department is going to a completely new version of CARD application with the
new masters of villages, the webland village masters and the LPM based market value
system as well as survey number based market value system with different prohibitory
masters checking system, the scrutiny of the document for market value, prohibitory
nature of property and payment of duties needs a careful examination before accepting
the document

Hence, it is the responsibility of the Sub Registrars to verify the market values,
prohibited properties, payment of duties and other statutory requirements. Any deviation
in this regard will be viewed seriously.

All the DIGS and DRs are instructed to train their staff particularly Senior Assistants,
Junior Assistants, SROS shall make use of user manual and ensure that the implementation
of CARD PRIMME is carried out smoothly and provide feedback from time to time basis
and provide feedback to this office as it is made live for all the SROS in the State as per
the schedule annexed to this Circular.

Encls: User Manual, Go Live Dates.

V Rama krishna
Commissioner and Inspector General of
Registration and Stamps, A.P., Vijayawada.
