GOVERNMENT OF ANDHRA PRADESH
ABSTRACT

Pensions - Andhra Pradesh Guaranteed Pension System (APGPS), 2023 — Orders Issued.
FINANCE (HR-III-PENSION & GPF) DEPARTMENT

G.0. Ms. No. 116 Date: 24.10.2023
Read the following:-

1. G.0. (P)No.88 Finance & Planning (FW. Pension-1) Department, dated 26.03.1980

2. G.0.Ms.No.653, Finance (Pension-1) Department, dated 22.09.2004

3. G.0.Ms.No.654, Finance (Pension-!) Department, dated 22.09.2004

4. G.0.Ms.No.655, Finance (Pension-l) Department, dated 22.09.2004

5. G.0.Ms.No. 121, Finance (HR.V-Pension, GPF) Department, dated 08.07.2017

6. G.0.Ms.No. 107, Finance (HR.V-Pension-|) Department, dated 29.06.2017

7. G.0.Ms.No.1747, G.A. (Cabinet-I) Department, dated 01.08.2019

8. G.0.Ms.No.2675, G.A. (Cabinet-|) Department, dated 27.11.2019

9. The Andhra Pradesh Guaranteed Pension System (APGPS) Act, 2023 (Act No.31

of 2023)
ORDER:

In the State of Andhra Pradesh, the pensionary benefits of the employees recruited on
or before 31-08-2004, are governed by the provisions of the Andhra Pradesh Revised
Pension Rules, 1980, Issued in the reference 1st read above, as amended from time
to time. The aforementioned Rules follow the framework of a ‘Defined Benefit’ (DB)
scheme.

2. The Government of India has replaced the existing Old Pension Scheme(OPS),
a ‘Defined Benefit’ scheme financed on a pay-as-you-go basis, with a "Defined
Contribution’ (DC) scheme viz. the New Pension Scheme (Contribution based
Pension Scheme), now called the National Pension System (NPS), for the Central
Government employees, vide the Notification No. 5/7/2003-ECB & PR, dated 22-12-
2003, of the Department of Economic Affairs, Ministry of Finance, Gol. The NPS was
made mandatory for all new recruits to the Central Government service (except the
armed forces) w.e.f. Ist January, 2004.

3. After the enactment of the PFRDA Act, 2013, as per Section 20 of the Act, the pension
scheme notified on 22-12-2003 has become the National Pension System (NPS)
under the Act. The NPS is now regulated under the PFRDA Act, 2013 & the regulation
framed thereunder by the Department of Financial Services and PFRDA.

 
 

The Government of Andhra Pradesh (GoAP), in the references 2nd to 4th read above,
has adopted the Government of India's National Pension System (NPS), also known
as the Contributory Pension Scheme (CPS), based upon the Defined Contribution
architecture, for the employees of the State recruited on or after 01-09-2004.
Further, in the reference 4th read above, the option to receive

Invalidation pension or family pension was extended in the case of premature exit
due to invalidation/ death & in the reference 5th read above, the benefit of retirement
gratuity/death gratuity was extended to the NPS subscribers.

After the adoption of the National Pension System (NPS) framework, the GoAP has
received several representations from the employees associations of the State
Government for de-adoption of CPS & restoration of OPS. To address the concerns
of employees, to review CPS Scheme, and to assess the options available, an Expert
Committee was constituted under the Chairmanship of Srl S.P. Tucker, IAS (Retd.),
former Chief Secretary to Government of Andhra Pradesh, vide the G.O. Rt. No. 2052,
Finance (HR-3-Pension-]] Department, dated 28-11-2018.

In the aforementioned context, the GoAP, in the reference 7th read above, has
constituted a Group of Ministers (GoM) to review the Expert Committee report and
furnish its recommendations to the Government for taking a decision in the matter, &
in the reference 8th read above, has also constituted a Working Committee to advise
the GoM. The GoAP has also engaged services of specialized agencies for undertaking
detailed studies in the matter. After a thorough examination of the various issues
by the GoM & the Working Committee, & multiple discussions with the Employees
Associations & other stakeholders, the GOAP has formulated the Andhra Pradesh
Guaranteed Pension System (APGPS), that seeks to replace the uncertainty of not
knowing what one would receive as pension from a fully market-linked CPS, with a
scheme where the pension is known during service and is guaranteed. To ensure the
financial security of the CPS employees, the Andhra Pradesh Guaranteed Pension
System (APGPS), also seeks to addresses certain genuine concerns of the current
CPS subscribers, which are not are not provisioned in the existing National Pension
System (NPS) framework, viz. spouse pension upon death of the retired APGPS
subscriber, cost of living adjustment, a monthly minimum guaranteed pension, &
health care scheme for the retired APGPS subscribers.

The Government, after a careful consideration of the entire matter, and keeping in
view the welfare of the employees under the Contributory Pension Scheme, while
also ensuring fiscal sustainability & inter-generational equity, hereby order to
implement the Andhra Pradesh Guaranteed Pension System, 2023, notified vide the
reference 9th read above, as detailed hereunder.

i. It shall extend to all the employees who are recruited on or after 1-9- 2004 and
whose pay and allowances are drawn from the Consolidated Fund of the State,
including all the tiers of all the Rural and Urban Local Bodies, Universities, etc.,
and have opted to subscribe to the Andhra Pradesh Guaranteed Pension System.

 

 

 
LC

It shall come into force with immediate effect.

The APGPS Subscriber shall be guaranteed the following benefits under the Act,
subject to the provisions of sub-section (4) of Section 6 of the Act.

a.

e.

Top-up amount to ensure a monthly Guaranteed Pension at the rate of fifty
percent (50%) of the last drawn basic pay, in case of a shortfall in the annuity
received by the retired APGPS Subscriber.

Top-up amount to ensure a monthly spouse pension at the rate of sixty
percent (60%) of the Guaranteed Pension, in case of a shortfall in the annuity
received by the spouse of the deceased APGPS Subscriber.

Cost of Living Adjustment on the last drawn basic pay as per inflation
adjusted dearness relief, as per the rules notified under this Act.

Top-up amount to ensure a monthly minimum Guaranteed Pension of
Rs.10,000/- (Rupees Ten thousand only) in case of short fall in the annuity
received by the APGPS subscriber.

Health Care Scheme for the retired APGPS subscribers.

iv. Conditions of eligibility for availing benefits under APGPS

 

1.

2.

3.

The APGPS Subscriber shall become eligible to receive the benefits under
the Guaranteed Pension System subject to sub- section (3) of Section 6 of
the Act and the following conditions:

a. Rendering of a minimum of ten (10) years of qualifying service, if retiring
on superannuation.

b. Rendering of a minimum of twenty (20) years of qualifying service, if
retiring from service on voluntary retirement.

c. Rendering of a minimum of thirty-three (33) years of qualifying service,
if retired by the Government in the public interest.

d. — Rendering of a minimum of ten (10) years of qualifying service, if retiring
from service on Medical Invalidation.

The APGPS Subscriber shall not be entitled to the benefits of APGPS in the
following situations.

a. _ Retired from service due to resignation.

b. Dismissal from service, removal from service & compulsory retirement
arising from disciplinary action.

If there are any disciplinary or judicial proceedings pending against the
APGPS subscriber at the time of retirement, the benefits specified under
Section 3 shall become payable only after conclusion of the disciplinary/
judicial proceedings, as per the rules notified under the Act.
&  ° .°.».»”»+x,xLEEZ 7

4. In case the APGPS subscriber dies while in service, the conferment of
benefits shall be as prescribed under the rules.

V. Registration Into APGPS.

1. The existing NPS subscribers shall exercise the option to subscribe to APGPS
within the period as prescribed in the rules under APGPS Act, 2023.

 

2. Employees who join service after notification of the Act shall exercise the
option to subscribe to the APGPS at the time of Joining the service.

3. The detailed process and procedure for APGPS Registration shall be detailed
in the Rules notified under this Act separately.

4. Subscribing to APGPS shall not affect in any way, the monthly contributions
made to the NPS by the employees and the employer as per the relevant
Rules and executive Instructions in force.

vi. Amount of Guaranteed Pension and the Methodology.

1. Upon retirement of the employee, the purchase of the annuity plan from
an Annuity Service Provider shall be made in accordance with the rules
notified under this Act, duly utilizing the accumulated pension corpus in the
individual pension account.

2. The Guaranteed Pension shall consist of the Annuity Component and the
Top-up Component as specified below:

a. The Annuity Component is the annuity receivable by the APGPS
subscriber based on the annuity plan selected as per sub-section (1) of
Section 6 above.

b. | Top-up Component is the amount receivable by the APGPS subscriber,
if any, so that the subscriber receives Guaranteed Pension as specifled
under this Act..

3. Calculation of Guaranteed Pension and the Top-up Component shall be as
prescribed in the rules notified under this Act.

4. The part withdrawals and the final withdrawal made by the APGPS
subscriber shall result in a proportional reduction in the Guaranteed Pension
as prescribed in the rules notified under this Act.

5. It shall be competent for the Government to withhold or withdraw the Top
Up component or a part therefrom, in accordance with the prescribed rules.

vii. Spouse Pension upon death of the retired APGPS subscriber.

1. In the case of death of the retired APGPS subscriber, the spouse shall
receive Spouse Pension at the rate of sixty (60%) of the Guaranteed Pension,
consisting of the Annuity Component and the Top-up Component as specified
below:

  
LC

8.

9.

a. The Annuity Component is the annuity receivable by the spouse based
on the annuity plan selected by the APGPS subscriber as per sub-section
(1) of Section 6 of the Act.

b. Top-up Component is the amount receivable by the spouse, so that he/
she receives the Spouse Pension as specified under the Act.

2. The process, procedure and conditions for determination, processing,
sanction, authorization & disbursal of the Spouse Pension shall be governed
by the prescribed rules under the APGPS Act, 2023.

viii. 1. The authorities concerned with registration, processing, sanction, authorizing
and disbursement of the benefits to the APGPS subscribers under the Act
shall be prescribed under the rules.

2. The Government shall designate an office to perform the functions of
record keeping, accounting, administration and grievance redress for APGPS
subscribers.

Government will issue the rules separately for implementation of the APGPS Act
2023 duly following the stipulated procedure.

The attention of the Collectors in the state is invited to various instances wherein it is
alleged that mutations are being carried out by the Tahsildars basing on true copies,
xerox copies and other unauthenticated documents submitted by the applicants that
 

 

& ° .»”»”»+x=xLEE 7

is leading to unnecessary litigation and the Revenue department is at the receiving
end for having carried out the mutation without properly verifying the documents and
the Hon'ble High court has commented adversely on the issue.

 

2) The Collectors are therefore instructed that:

(a) In every case of mutation, it shall be mandatory for the Tahsildars to verify
all original documents such as original sale deed(Except in cases where the
Document is viewed after obtaining the same online through an API from the
Server of the Registration Dept.), original documents of link, original EC and
other relevant document.

(b) For each case of mutation, Tahsildar concerned shall certify online that she has
verified the required documents in original, and not merely copies(photo copies/
true copies)

(c) No mutation or any orders shall be passed without verifying the original
documents and documents which are acceptable under the Indian Evidence Act.

3) Any deviation will be viewed seriously and District Collectors are requested to take
necessary action to disseminate these instructions to last functionary in the Revenue
machinery and ensure strict compliance.

4) It is to also informed that Act 15 of 2022, specifically Section 5, empowers the
District Collectors to initiate proceedings under criminal law in case any application
for amendment of R.O.R. under this act is made fraudulently.

THE ANDHRA PRADESH GAZETTE

PART IV-B EXTRAORDINARY PUBLISHED BY AUTHORITY
No. 351 AMARAVATI. FRIDAY, 27 OCTOBER, 2023.

ANDHRA PRADESH ACTS, ORDINANCES AND REGULATIONS Etc.,
The following Act of the Andhra Pradesh Legislature received the assent of the
Governor on the 18" October, 2023 and the said assent is hereby first published on the 27
October, 2023 in the Andhra Pradesh Gazette for general information:

ACT No. 35 of 2023.

AN ACT FURTHER TO AMEND THE ANDHRA PRADESH ASSIGNED LANDS (PROHIBITION
OF TRANSFERS) ACT 1977.

Be it enacted by the legislature of the State of Andhra Pradesh in the Seventy-fourth
Year of Republic of India as follows,-

Short Title extent and commencement

1. (1) This Act may be called the Andhra Pradesh Assigned Lands (Prohibition of
Transfers) (Amendment) Act, 2023.

(2) It extends to the whole of the State of Andhra Pradesh.

(3) Sections 2 and 3 shall be deemed to have come into force with effect on and
from the 31st July, 2023 and Section 4 shall be deemed to have come into force
with effect on and from 21 January, 1977.

 
 

& °. °.»”»+x,x}FEZ 7

Amendment of Section 2 Act 9 of 1977.

2 — In the Andhra Pradesh Assigned Lands (Prohibition of Transfers) Act, Amendment
1977, (hereinafter referred to as the principal Act); in Section 2, in of Section 2
clause (1) after the words, ‘subject to the condition of non-alienation, the words,
‘within a period of twenty (20) years from the date of assignment in the case of lands
assigned for agricultural purpose, and within a period of ten (10) years from the date
of assignment in case of house sites, shall be inserted,

 

Amendment of Section 3

3. In the Principal Act, in Section 3, for sub-section (2), the following sub-section shall
be substituted, namely:-

"(2) (i) No landless poor person or his / her legal heir, shall transfer any land assigned
for agricultural purpose, and no person shall acquire any such assigned land,
either by purchase, gift, lease (except in the case of Lease to the Andhra Pradesh
Green Energy Corporation Ltd., for use as deemed fit and including for usage of
non-agriculture purpose)", mortgage, exchange or otherwise, within a period of
twenty (20) years from the date of assignment.

(ii) After expiry of the above period of twenty years, such assigned land may be
alienated by the assignee or his/her legal heirs as per the procedure prescribed.
without further reference to the government.

(iii) where any land adjoining such assigned land is earmarked for any use other than
agriculture use in the master plan of the area, the assignee or his/her legal heirs
shall be paid current basic value of said adjoining non agricultural land by the
purchaser of the such assigned land.".

Amendment of Section 4

4. In the Principal Act, in Section 4, in sub-section (1), after clause (c). the following
clause shall be inserted, namely: -

(d) Not withstanding anything contained in any other law for the time being in force,
where an assigned land is resumed in public interest for a public purpose, the
assignee shall be entitled to payment of compensation equivalent to the market
value of the land on par with land owner, but shall not claim for payment of
compensation amount as per the negotiated award with the land owner or
amount awarded by the reference or appellate courts."

Repeal and savings Ordinance No 9 of 2023

(1) The Andhra Pradesh Assigned Lands (Prohibition of Transfers) (Amendment)
Ordinance, 2023 is hereby repealed.

2

   
Se)

(2) Notwithstanding such repeal, anything done or any action taken under the said
Ordinance shall be deemed to have been done or taken under this Act.

The attention is invited to the subject cited. A New version of CCA i.e. CARD PRIMME
application is developed and is ready for implementation. The CARD PRIMME application
is developed to address the current challenges in the present software like scalability
and adaptability to modern technologies. The present CARD PRIMME software is designed
to have a good citizen Interface by separating the front end activities of citizen from

the backend activities of the Department officials. The citizen interface is designed in
a simple Application form with a tracking service to submit his application to any Sub

sak WN

  
i

registrar office of his choice or to the office of VSWS where the property is situated. The
citizen can submit the Application from through a web portal. In order to simplify the
entire registration service the citizen is provided with a facility of document generation
online through the system. After entering the property detalls, citizen also can get the
chargeable value of the property based on the market value guidelines values of that
locality. The party can pay the duties online through payment gate way integrated in
the application itself. Payments can also be made through CFMS or E-stampting/Stock
Holding Corporation of India Ltd. The citizen can book a slot online in an office of his choice
to get speedy and better services.

The citizen or his representative after submitting his application online visits the
office on the date and time as reserved in the slot. The sub registrar after scrutiny makes
minor changes to the data, if required only after the approval of the executing parties and
accepts the application on satisfaction that there are no impediments for registration of
the document. A check-slip will be generated, thumb impression, e-sign of the parties and
witnesses will be captured and the process of registration will be completed by the sub
registrar. The document can be retrieved by any sub registrar in the state or meeseva /
CSC centers and certified copies can also be issued.

The CARD 2.0 software is already deployed in Districts of NTR and Krishna. All the
joint sub registrars and VSWS officers in the Districts of Anakapalli Nandyal, Tirupati and
Visakapatnam are directed to use CARD 2.0 application with effect from 06.11.2023.

undersigned immediately.

Circular Memo No.E1/3945/2023, Dt.28-11-2023

Sub: PUBLIC SERVICE REGISTRATION AND STAMPS DEPARTMENT — Sanction of all
kinds of leave Certain Instruction — Regarding.

OK

It has come to the notice of the undersigned that, some of the staff members are
applying long leave on medical grounds and other grounds stating false content of
subject and disease for their own cause of interest ignoring the exigency of public
services. Some are proceeding on leave and extend leave without prior sanction
and proper relief by the competent authority, which causing dislocation of work
and service delivery to public. Keeping them in view, the following instructions are
issued;

 
& °».».».”»”»«+x,xFEZ 7

a) As per the provisions made in F.R. 67 and A.P.Leave Rules, 1933, ‘Leave’ cannot
be claimed as a matter of right. When exigencies of public services so require,
discretion to refuse or revoke leave of any descriptions is reserved with the
sanctioning authority.

 

b) For availing leave prior sanction and proper relief is necessary and also proper
handing over of charge required.

c) The Medical Certificate in support the leave by the Gazetted Officers shall be from
a Government Medical Officer, not below the rank of a Civil Surgeon, however, in
the case of Non-Gazetted Officers, the Medical Certificate can be obtained from
the Asst. Civil Surgeon.

d) Keeping in view the exigency of workload in the department, cases of extending
leave on medical grounds beyond one month shall be referred to the Medical
Board concerned for their verification and recommendations on the trueness of
the disease mentioned in the certificate.

e) As per orders issued in G.0.Ms.No. 48, GA(Ser.A)Dept., dated. 18.02.2003, the
Government Employees who produce and submit false medical certificates
for the purpose of long leave and transfers, and also for getting benefits under
Disability programme are entitled to Impose major penalty of removal from
service, under A.P.C.S(CC&A) Rules, 1991 after following due procedure. The
Medical Officers, who issue Incorrect and false Medical Certificate also to get
major penalty of removal from service.

Absence from duty without leave is misconduct on the part of the employee for
which disciplinary action can be taken against him/her by the competent authority
as per the procedure laid down in Andhra Pradesh Civil Services (CCA) rules, 1991.

2. All the Leave Sanctioning Authorities and staff members of the department are
therefore, directed to follow the above instructions scrupulously before sanctioning
and going on any kind of leave. The cadre controlling authorities are also directed
to bring it to the notice of all the staff members under their control for strict
implementation. They shall ensure that every employee adheres to the instructions
in the matter. Any violations in this regard will be viewed seriously and to initiate
disciplinary action as per Rules in vogue.

3. All the leave sanctioning authorities shall exercise utmost care while sanctioning
and extending long leave to the staff and also review the existing long leaves already
sanctioned to the staff and still continuing if any keeping in view the above guidelines.

4. Any deviation in the matter will be viewed seriously.

V Rama krishna
Commissioner and Inspector General of
Registration and Stamps, A.P., Tadepalli

GOVERNMENT OF ANDHRA PRADESH
ABSTRACT

Registration & Stamps Department Amendments to the Andhra Pradesh Hindu
Marriage Registration Rules, 1965 Notification Orders - Issued.

REVENUE(REGN.II) DEPARTMENT
G.0.Ms.No. 609 Dated:22.12.2023

Read the following:
1. G.0.Ms.No.654, Home ( General) Dept., dt. 22-03-1965.

2. CIRCULAR No: CARD -HM/SM- 1/2023, Dated: 31.12.2022 From the Commissioner
and Inspector General of Registration and Stamps, Vijayawada.

3. From the Commissioner and Inspector General of Registration and Stamps,
Vijayawada Letter No.BDM/68612023, Dated: 06.03.2023 & 25.04.2023.

OK
ORDER:

The Registration and Stamps Department registers marriages under the Hindu
Marriage Act, 1955. Hitherto, it has been done manually and parties wishing to get
the marriages registered ought to approach the Sub Registrar offices under whose
jurisdiction either the bride or groom resided for a period of 6 months prior to the
date of marriage or the jurisdiction of the Sub Registrar where the marriage gets
solemnized. Many representations were received by the Department to allow
registration of Hindu marriages in any of the Sub Registrar Offices in the State
without restricting the parties to go to the Sub Registrar Offices as mentioned above.
The reason put forth by the parties is that they had to move to different places on
account of transfers in both the public and private sectors and the movement of
the general public in search of livelihood. An overwhelming number of people have
requested making the marriage registration process online in view of the improved
digital literacy and access to high-speed internet services.

2. Keeping in view of the suggestions received from the public, the C&IG, Registration
formed a committee to study the feasibility of implementation of these suggestions
and submitted recommendations to the Government.

3. The above suggestions of the public have been examined keeping in view the feasibility
of implementation and the public good that it brings about.

 
& ° .»”»;x,4}FE 7

4. The Government after careful examination of the matter have decided to allow the
process of registration of marriages in the digital form also and accordingly hereby
make the amendments to the Andhra Pradesh Hindu Marriage Registration Rules,
1965 Issued in G.0.Ms.No.654, Home (General) Dept, dated the 22nd March, 1965
to make them consistent with the proposed changes in the marriage registration
process.

 

5. The following notification will be published in an Extraordinary issue of the Andhra
Pradesh Gazette dated 22.12.2023.

NOTIFICATION

In exercise of the powers conferred under sub-section (1) of section 8 of the Hindu
Marriage Act, 1955 (Act No.25 of 1955), the Government of Andhra Pradesh here by
makes the following amendments to the Andhra Pradesh Hindu Marriage Registration
Rules, 1965 issued in G.O.Ms. No.654, Home (General) Dept., dated the 22nd March, 1965
as subsequently amended:

AMENDMENTS
In the said rules,
1. in rule 2,
(1) for clause (c), the following shall be substituted, namely,-

(c) "Form" means a form appended to these rules and maintained either in physical
or in electronic form as defined in the Information Technology Act, 2000.".

(ii) in clause (f), the words “either in physical or in electronic form" shall be added
at the end.

(lil) for clause (h), the following shall be substituted, namely,-

(h) “Registrar General" means the Inspector General of Registration appointed by
the Government under section 3 of the Registration Act, 1908 read with section
6(1) (b) of the Births, Deaths and Marriages Registration Act 1886 (Central Act
6 of 1886) or section 2(1) (b) of the Andhra Pradesh (T.A Registrar General of
Births, Deaths and Marriages Act, 1953 (Act Vill of 1953).”.

2. in rule 4,

(i) in sub-rule(1), the words “either in physical or in electronic form” shall be
added at the end.

(ii) for sub-rule(2), the following shall be substituted, namely,-

(2) The Hindu Marriage Register shall be maintained either in physical or in
electronic form having been machine numbered consecutively.".

  
   

3. In rule 5, for sub-rule(1), the following shall be substituted, namely,-

(1) An application for the registration of a Hindu Marriage, shall be submitted
in Form ‘A’ before any Registrar in the State and shall be signed including
e-sign by each party to the marriage either in manual form or in electronic
form and shall present in person before the same Registrar in the State.

Provided that an application for the registration of marriage, solemnized beyond the
territories of India may be presented within one month from the date on which the
parties arrive in the State of Andhra Pradesh before any Registrar in the said State of
Andhra Pradesh.".

4. Inrule 6, for sub-rule(3), the following shall be substituted, namely,-

3) On being satisfied about the marriage, the Registrar shall enter the particulars
of the marriage in his own hand or electronically in the Hindu Marriage Register.
Every entry relating to such particulars shall be signed by both the parties to the
marriage either manually or in electronic form.

Provided that the Registrar may in his discretion require that one or more witnesses
shall also sign either manually electronically in the Hindu Marriage Register.”. or In

5. for rule 8, the following shall be substituted, namely,-

8. (1) Every erasure or interlineation occurred in making entries in the Hindu
Marriage Register shall be attested by the Registrar and a note of the number of
erasures and interlineations in the entries shall be made at the foot of the page
containing the entries by the Registrar in his own hand or electronically also
attested with his initials. He shall then authenticate the entries with his signature
and date and in case of Hindu Marriage Register, maintained in electronic form
any correction shall be done by the Registrar, the reasons so recorded may be
substantiated. Electronic log of the changes shall be preserved.

(2) No correction or alteration in material particulars like name including surname
or any clerical or typographical error regarding age, date or place shall be made
in the Hindu Marriage Register either in manual form or in electronic form,
without obtaining the sanction of the inspecting officer and District Registrar of
Assurances concerned.

(3) Every correction made after obtaining the required sanction under sub rule (2)
shall be made by the Registrar, by a note in either physical form or electronic
form in the foot note without any alteration of the original entry and shall be
signed electronically or physically and dated by him.".

6. In rule 9, the words "either in manual form or in electronic form" shall be added at
the end.

7. Inrule 10, after the expression "Form C", the words "either manually or in electronic

form" shall be inserted.

i. for sub-rule (1), (2) and (3), the following shall be substituted, namely,-

(1) Every application for grant of copies of records or for an extract from the

duly paying required fee prescribed.

2) Certified extracts from the Hindu Marriage Register and certified coples of

(3) Table of Fees:

REGISTRATION OF HINDU MARRIAGES UNDER MARRIAGE ACT, 1955.
[G.0. MS. NO. 654, HOME (GENERAL-A) 2 MARCH 1965]

In exercise of the powers conferred by section 8 of the Hindu Marriage Act, 1955
(Central Act 25 of 1955) the Governor of Andhra Pradesh hereby makes the following
rules, the same having been previously published for general information.-

RULES

1. These rules may be called the Andhra Pradesh Hindu Marriage Registration
Rules, 1965.

2. In these rules, unless the context otherwise requires -
(a) "Act" means the Hindu Marriage Act, 1955 [Central Act 25 of 1955J.

(b) "Compulsory registration area" means the area in which registration of
marriages is directed by the Government to be compulsory under sub section
(2) of section 8

(c) "Form" means a form appended to these rules:
(d) “Government” means the Government of Andhra Pradesh

(e) "Hindu Marriage" means a marriage including remarriage solemnized in
accordance with the provisions of the Act

Registrar General of Births, Deaths and Marriages Act. 1953 (Act VIll of 1953);

(1) "Registrar" means a Registrar appointed for registering Hindu Marriages
under the Act.

(j) "Section" means a Section of the Act

3. (1) The Government may, by notification published in the Andhra Pradesh Gazette
appoint as many persons as may be necessary as Registrars for the purpose of
registering the Hindu marriages under the Act, with jurisdiction over such area
as may be specified in the notification.

 
&  ° °»”»;+x,x}FE 7

(2) Every Registrar shall reside within the local limits of his jurisdiction and shall
cause his name, designation and the working hours of his office to be written
in English. Telugu and in the regional language of the area and displayed in a
conspicuous part of the outer side of the building in which the e office is located.

 

4. (1) AHindu Marriage which has been solemnized may be registered by the Registrar
in the Hindu Marriage Register which shall be maintained by him in Form "B”

(2) The Hindu Marriage Register shall be a bound volume of one hundred leaves the
pages having been machine numbered consecutively.

5. (1) An application for the registration of a Hindu Marriage, shall be in Form ‘A’ and
shall be signed by each party to the marriage [*] and shall be presented in person
before the Registrar in whose jurisdiction the marriage is solemnized or before
the Registrar in whose jurisdiction either party to the marriage has been residing
for at least six months immediately preceding the date of marriage:

[*] Omitted vide G.O.MS. No 7 Women's Development, child welfare & Labour
(Programs) Development dt 8.1.1993.

Provided that an application for the registration of marriage, solemnized
beyond the territories of India excluding the State of Jammu and Kashmir may
be presented within one month from the date on which the parties arrive in
the State of Andhra Pradesh before any Registrar in the said State of Andhra
Pradesh.

(2) It shall be attested by any one of the following persons if any applicant is illiterate:
(1) a Village Munsif
(ii) a Magistrate of any class including an Honorary Magistrate

(iii) a Government servant whose emoluments are not less than Rs. 50 per
month:

(iv) a Government pensioner whose pension is not less than Rs. 25 per
month

(v) amember of a Zilla Parishad, a Panchayat Samithi, or a Grama Panchayat:
(vi) a member of a Municipal Council or a Municipal Committee

(vii) any person authorised to solemnize a marriage under the Special
Marriage Act, 1954 (Central Act 43 of 1954)

(viii) A Medical Officer in Government, Local Fund or Municipal employment
or a private medical practitioner holding a University diploma or degree
recognised by the Government;

(ix) a member of the Parliament or of the Andhra Pradesh State Legislature.

  
LC!

*+(3) It shall be presented within the month from the date of solemnisation of the
marriage:

Provided that if owing to urgent necessity or unavoidable accident it is not so
presented within the prescribed period, the Registrar may, if satisfied with
the reasons adduced for the failure to present it within the prescribed period,
condone the delay not exceeding one month.

Provided further that the Registrar General may condone the delay beyond the
period of one month.)

#* Omitted vide G.0.MS.No. 274 Revenue (Regn-Il) Dept Dt 27.4.2012.

6. (1) On receipt of the application in Form ‘A’ the Registrar shall unless both the
parties to the marriage appear before him personally and are identified to his
satisfaction give notice of the application to the other party or parties and make
such enquiries of summary character as he thinks fit regarding the mariage.

(2) Evidence if any required by the Registrar shall be taken by him on oath

Provided that the examination of paradanashin ladies who do not appear before
the Registrar may be conducted through a Hammamnee or Mana

(3) On being satisfied about the marriage the Registrar shall enter the particulars of
the marriage in his own hand in the Hindu Marriage Register. Every entry relating
to such particulars shall be signed by both the parties to the marriage.

Provided that the Registrar may in his discretion require that one or more
witnesses shall also sign in the Hindu Marriage Register:

7. (1) When the Registrar is not satisfied about the identity of the parties or about
the fact of the marriages, he shall by on order in writing refuse to register a
marriage and shall record the reasons for his decision.

(2) An appeal against such order of the Registrar shall lie to the Registrar General
who may pass suitable orders after giving an opportunity to both the parties and
his order shall be final.

8. (1) Every erasure or interlineation occurred in making entries in the Hindu Marriage
Register shall be attested by the Registrar and a note of the number of erasures
and interlineations in the entries shall be made at the foot of the page containing
the entries by the Registrar in his own hand and attested with his initials. He
shall then authenticate the entries with his signature and date.

(2) “No correction or atteration in material particulars like name including surname
or any clerical or typographical error regarding age, date or place shall be made
in the Hindu Marriage Registrar, without obtaining the sanction of the Inspecting
Officer and District Registrar Assurances concerned".

substituted vide G.OMS.No 274, Revenue (Regn-ll) Dept, dt 27.04.2012.

——

 
& °» .»”»”»«;x,xLEEZ 7

(3) Every correction made after obtaining the required sanction under sub-rule (2)
shall be made by the Registrar by a note in the footnote, without any alteration
of the original entry, and shall be signed and dated by him.

 

9.  Allapplications for registration of Hindu Marriages and for copies of certified extracts
from the Hindu Marriage Register shall be serially numbered separately for each
calendar year, and preserved as a permanent record.

10. Every Registrar shall cause to be maintained indices in Form 'C of all entries made
in the Hindu Marriage Register. Every entry in an index shall be made alphabetically
with reference to the surname of the party wherever it is given and in other cases
with reference to the name of the party.

11. The Hindu Marriage Register shall at all reasonable times be open for inspection in
the presence of the Registrar by any person applying to inspect it

12. The Registrar-General may authorise any officer to be an Inspecting Officer for the
purpose of inspecting the Hindu Marriage Register and other marriage records.

13. The Inspecting Officer shall inspect the marriage records of Hindu Marriages and
submit an inspection report in duplicate to the Registrar- General

14. The Registrar General shall scrutinise the report and forward a copy thereof to the
Registrar with his further remarks if any added on them through the Inspecting
Officer concerned.

15. (1) Every application for grant of copies of records or for an extract from the Hindu
Marriage Registrar may be made either in person or by post addressed to the
Registrar, with the court fee stamp duly affixed and accompanied by such
number of stamps as are necessary to prepare the required copy and in the case
of a request for grant of extract from the Hindu Marriage Register a sum of Rs. 5
being the fee

(2) Certified extracts from the Hindu Marriage Register and certified copies of other
records shall be granted under the official seal of the Registrar on payment of
the fees.

(3) In addition to the fees prescribed in sub-rule (1). The following fees shall be
levied by the Marriage Registrar.

  
Se

17. A receipt in "Form-E" shall be granted for the fee paid in person under rule 15

 

18. (1) The following records shall be maintained by the Registrar:
(a) Applications made for registration of Hindu Marriages or for correction of
together with concerned records

(b) Hindu Marriage Register.

(c) File of applications for certified extracts,

(d) Register of fees.

(e) Chalans for the money remitted into the treasury.

(f) Fee Receipt Book

(g) General correspondence

h) File of G.Os and Circulars.
(1) Indices.
(2) The records referred to in clauses (a), (b), (c), (d), fe), (g), (h) and (1) of
sub-rule (1) shall be preserved permanently.

19. The Registrar-General may specify any other records to be main- tained by the
Registrar and determine the period of preservation of such records.

20. (1) Any Registrar, who discovers any error in the form or substance of any entry in
the Hindu Marriages Register may, within one month next after the discovery
of such error in the presence of the persons married and in case of their death
or absence, in the presence of two other credible witnesses and subject to the
provisions contained in rule 8 direct the correction of the error.

Provided that, where any such correction was made in the absence of the
persons married, the nature of such correction shall be intimated to them by
registered post with acknowledgement due al their last known address

(2) Every correction made under this rule shall be attested by the parties or the
witnesses as the case may be, in whose presence it is made.

21. (1) For the registration of a marriage, the Registrar may, upon being provided with a
conveyance, attend the Jail situate within his jurisdiction if one of the parties is
confined therein under civil or criminal process or the residence of the persons
who reside within his jurisdiction and who are exempt by law from personal
appearance in Court under Code of Civil Procedure, 1908, namely;

(a) women who, according to the customs and manners, of the country, ought
not to be compelled to appear in public, and

(b) persons of rank specially exempted by the Government or persons who by
reason of bodily infirmity are unable without risk or serious appear at the
office of the Registrar inconvenience to appear at the office of the Registrar.

(ii) A list of persons exempted under clause (b) above, shall be obtained by
the Registrar-General from the High Court and communicated to the
Registrars.

GOVERNMENT OF ANDHRA PRADESH
REGISTRATION AND STAMPS DEPARTMENT
CIRCULAR No:G1/1561/2020, Dated: 04.12.2023

Sub: R&S, Dept. CCA Development of new application, CARD 2.0 and its deployment
in the state Officers from the field offices seeking clarification regarding
obtaining Aadhar numbers of the parties to the documents executed/in favour
of NRI's and Citizens of Indian Origin certain clarification issued Regarding.

Ref: 1) Memo No. CARD/e-2012791/2021, dt. 26-10-2023

2) D.O.Letter No. 15028/1/2020-DBE-HQ-HO dt. 06-02-2023 of the Chief
Executive Officer, UIDAI, New Delhi.

The attention is invited to the subject cited. They are informed that, a new version
of software application, CARD 2.0 duly incorporating the process of mutation of
properties is developed and deployed in the state and the registration and other
services provided by the department are being delivered using the new application.
In the new application, Aadhar numbers of the parties to the documents are required
to prove the identity of the parties on voluntary basis.

2. It is further, informed that many registering officers are expressing doubts in relation
to obtaining Aadhar numbers of the parties to various documents presented before
them for registration. Most of the queries are regarding NRIs and citizens of Indian
origin. As per aadhaar authorities there is no bar for issuing Aadhar card to NRIs and
citizens of Indian origin. Aadhar cards are issued to even foreign citizens if they live
in India for 182 days within a span of 365 days.

3. Vide reference 2nd cited the CEO, UAID requested the states that "Aadhaar based
biometric authentication of buyers, sellers and witnesses may be incorporated in the
land/property registration process”.

4. It is also informed that, registration of documents relating to transfer of immovable
properties of both agricultural and non-agricultural classifications is to be followed
by auto mutation of the immovable properties in the name of the transferees.
Auto mutation of the property is carried out automatically through the CARD 2.0
application, the moment the document is registered.

5. It has also come to the notice of undersigned that fraudulent transactions have
occurred by impersonation of the registering parties mostly masquerading as NRIs.

6. It is, therefore, clarified that Aadhar authentication is required on voluntary basis
both for the transferor and transferee in relation to documents effecting transfers

271

 
 

&  ° .»”»+x,x}FEZ 7

of immovable properties of all classifications both for NRIs/Citizens of Indian origin
and resident citizens for completing the process of registration. If any party to a
document refuses to share Aadhar number, the person may be enlightened that the
department cannot effect auto mutation of the property.

 

Sd/-V.RAMAKRISHNA
Commissioner and Inspector General of
Registration and Stamps, A.P., Tadepalli.

Sub: Registration & Stamps Department Entries in the Webland- Tahsildars
issuing certification for rectification of entries- Such changes are to be
made in Webland through Mutation process- Tahsildars and other Revenue
Department officers Shall NOT accept such manual certificates- Instructions

OK

The attention of the all Registering Officers in the State is invited through the subject
and references cited. They are instructed that, the CCLA and Special Chief Secretary
Revenue department, A.P issued certain Instructions to the Revenue department officials
that "all Tahsildars and others and Revenue officers are instructed expressly to NOT issue
rectification manual certificates and citizens should be informed that, any applications for
correction of revenue record shall be made in the Grama/Ward Sachivalayams as such
services are already available". A copy of the reference cited is enclosed.

In this regard, all Registering Officers are instructed NOT to accept any manual
certificates of correction of Revenue records and electronic records are only to be relied
on. Any violation of these instructions would be viewed seriously including disciplinary
action.

CCLA ref.No.LR/551/2021, dt:#Approved Date#

Sub: Lands. Entries in the webland Tahsildars issuing certification for rectification
of entries - Such changes are to be made in webland through Mutation Process
Tahsidars and other Revenue Dept officers- Shall not Issue rectification
Certificates - Instructions issued - Reg. (E 1757616)

Ref: — 1.. Commissioner, IGRS Circular memo no CARD/C-1730463, dt: 30.03.2023
2.. CCLA's Ref.No. REV02-16022/292/2023-LC-2282209, dt:30/11/2023

OK

The Commissioner & Inspector General, Registration and Stamps has Informed that,
citizens are approaching Registration Officers with physical certificates issued by certain
Tahsildars certifying that corrections have been carried out in Revenue records, without
actually carrying out the certified changes in the electronic Revenue records. Any change
in the revenue records shall mandatorily have to carried out in the electronic revenue
records in accordance with the AP Rights in Land and Pattadar passbooks Act (ROR) Act
and Rules.

All the District Collectors are aware that instructions have already been issued that,
any and all amendments to the electronically maintained Revenue records, shall be carried
out strictly in accordance with the process prescribed in the ROR Act and Rules. In view
of the these instructions and the Law in force. Tahsildars and other Revenue officers in
the state are barred from issuing manual certificates regarding the corrections/changes
in webland without actually carrying out such changes in the electronically maintained
revenue records. Any such certificate shall have to be obtained by citizens from an extract
of the Revenue Records at the Grama/Ward sachivalayams or by downloading them in
their own computers.

The Collectors are informed that such issue of manual certificates certifying
corrections in adangal/other records corrections is expressly barred and is to be taken
seriously. All Tahsildars and other Revenue Officers are instructed expressly to NOT issue
such manual certificates. Citizens should be informed that any application for correction
of Revenue records shall pe made in the Grama/Ward sachivalayams as such services are
already available.

Any violation of these instructions would be viewed seriously, and necessary action,
disciplinary and under punitive, shall be promptly initiated as already directed by the
Hon'ble Lokayukta and instructed in the reference 2nd cited above.

 
 

&  °» ».»”»”»«+x%4TEZ

The Collectors are requested to disseminate these instructions to all the Revenue
functionaries in the District and ensure strict compliance.

 

The Commissioner, Registration and Stamps may issue instructions to all his staff to
NOT to accept such manual certificates of correction of Revenue records with immediate
effect.

CCLA's Ref. No. REV02-16022/292/2023-LC-2282209, dt:30/11/2023

Sub: CCLA- Legal Cell Institution of Lokayukta, Kurnool- Complaint No 1588/2021/
B2 Hon'ble Lokayukta issued orders to initiate criminal prosecution against
the persons accountable for tampering of records etc and also those persons
whose names are recorded as pattedar and enjoyer Request to comply the
orders - Reg.

Ref:- Deputy Registrar, Institution Lokayukta, AP, Kurnool Lr. Dis. No. Ref 1588/2021/
B2/LOK/10939/2023, dt. 08.11.2023.

OK

The attention of all the District Collectors / Sub-Collectors / RDOs in the State is
invited to the reference cited, wherein Hon'ble Institution of Lokayukta, AP, Kurnool on a
complaint filed by certain agriculturists with regard to encroachment of 'Vanka Poramboke’
in Amakathadu (V), Krishnagiri Mandal have observed that-

"Several instances came to the notice of this Institution that the officials of the
office of the Tahsildar, including Tahsildars are meddling / tampering with the revenue
records in collusion with the private persons and they are entering the name of the
private person in webland adangals in respect of the lands classified as water bodies, hill
porambokes, unobjectionable land, sometimes even in respect of Sy.Nos of R&B roads,
rivulets, streams, village grazing lands for their wrong full gain perhaps, the Tahsildar
level officers are daringly Indulging into such activities. Further the private persons seem
to be encouraging the Tahsildar under the notion that no severe action will be taken even
though their irregularity / corrupt practice is detected in future. Even the slackness of the
authorities in identifying the officials accountable for the irregular entries is aggravating
the issue. Therefore, it is a high time for the authorities to issue necessary instructions

274

  
to all the District Collectors, including the Revenue Divisional Officer, authorizing them
to initiate the criminal prosecution against the persons accountable for such activities
whenever this type of tampering of revenue records is detected instead of mere rectifying
the entries. Without such serious action against such unethical practice by the erring
officers, such activities cannot be curtailed. In this particular case the action taken against
the culprits by the public official is highly appreciated by this Institution. Mere rectification
of the entries do not deter the erring officials. Only stringent action against erring officers
after thorough enquiry would curtail such activities so that the government lands and
water bodies which are valuable wealth of the state are protected from encroachers.”

and directed to issue instructions to the District Collector, RDOs to initiate disciplinary
action promptly as and when this type of tampering of revenue records is detected and
apart from that they may also instruct the District Collectors and Revenue Divisional
Officers to initiate criminal prosecution against those officers who supervise the entry
of the details who have allowed the tampering the valuable revenue records related to
the Government lands including water bodies, water streams etc., and also against those
whose names have been entered as pattadars and enjoyers in respect of subject land.

In view of the above, all the District Collectors, the Sub-Collectors / Revenue
Divisional Officers in the State are hereby directed to Initiate disciplinary action promptly
as and when this type of tampering of revenue records is detected and apart from this
they are also directed to initiate criminal prosecution against those officers who supervise
the entry of the details who have allowed the tampering the valuable revenue records
related to the Government lands including water bodies, water streams etc., and also
against those whose names have been entered as pattadars and enjoyers in respect of
subject land.

In exercise of the powers conferred under the Registration Act, 1908 the Governor of
Andhra Pradesh hereby amend the following Rules under the Registration Act, 1908 for
Electronic Thumb Impression Register.

AMENDMENT

The following amendments to rules 63 to 69 of Andhra Pradesh Rules under the

GOVERNMENT OF ANDHRA PRADESH
ABSTRACT

Registration & Stamps Department CARD Project providing infrastructural

improvements and arranging appropriate facilities to the public Creation of S-CARD
Society under the Andhra Pradesh Societies Registration Act, 2001 (Act No.35 of 2001) -
Permission accorded - Orders - Issued.

REVENUE (REGN.I) DEPARTMENT

G.0.Ms.No.597 Dated:20.12.2023
Read the following:

1. G.0.Ms.No. 170, Finance & Planning (FW.BG) Dept., dt: 23-04-01.

2. G.0.Ms.No.895, Rev (Regn-1) Dept., dated: 27-04-2001 (GO Ms.No.24, IT&C Dept,
Dt: 31.03.2001.

3. From the Commissioner and Inspector General (R&S), D.O.Lr.No.CARD4/2370/99,

Dated: 07-07-2001.

G.0.Ms.No.576, Revenue (Regn.I) Department, dt. 29-08-2001.

Lr.No.CARD/e-1373901/2021, Dt: 30.11.2021 from the Commissioner and

Inspector General of Registration and Stamps

NOP

ORDER:

2)

3)

 

The Government in the reference 1st read above, a detailed guidelines were Issued
for levying and utilization of User Charges for providing Improved quality of public
services. In the reference 2nd read above, orders were issued constituting a
committee to fix the appropriate User Charges on services rendered under the CARD
project and also laid the procedures to utilize the same. Accordingly, Government
accorded permission to C&IG, R&S, A.P. to levy and collect user charges. The user
charges have been revised from time to time vide reference Sth and 6th read above.

Levy and collection of user charges in the Registration department was intended to
maintain and finance e-Governance projects- CARD and to meet the recurring cost
associated with the CARD project such as upgradation of software, maintenance of
hardware, recurring expenditure, security, consumables, stationary etc.

The Commissioner and Inspector General has informed that, though a detailed
procedure was laid out, the amounts levied and collected user charges are not
flowing to the department and the lack of sufficient resources has severely crippled
the maintenance, upgradation and sustainment of CARD project. The user charges
are being deposited to the treasury account and not flowing to the department as
 

4)

5)

envisaged in the reference 1st read above. He has also informed that, CARD project
has become obsolete due to lack of funds and budgetary allocations. This is critically
effecting the service delivery to registering public.

The Commissioner & IG, A.P. has further requested to accord permission to enhance
the levy of User charges and utilize the enhanced funds by the Registration
department directly by the department under the aegis of a S-CARD society. He
has also submitted the draft proposals containing by-laws and MOA of the S-CARD
society for approval with the following objectives.

a) It shall assess the needs of the registering public and recommend to IGRS for
maintenance and upgrade of the e-governance systems. In the R&S Department
and repairs and modernization of Registration offices.

b) The society shall have a minimum of 7 members including a representative from
Finance department (Government).

c) It shall have a General Council as Apex body and Executive committee to maintain
its affairs.

d) The Society shall cause accounts to be kept of all its money and properties in
respect of its affairs.

e) A registered Chartered Accountant appointed by the General Council shall audit
annual accounts of S-CARD AP every year or by any chartered accountant by the
Inspector General of Registration or Government.

f) The Annual Report of the activities of S-CARD AP including all works undertaken
and various achievements made, during a year together with the Audited Annual
Accounts (Viz., Receipts and Payments Accounts, Income and Expenditure
Accounts and Balance Sheet) for the year, the Audit Certificate, the Audit Report
and replies of S-CARD AP to the observations contained in the Audit Report shall
be placed before the General Council at its next annual general meeting, through
the Executive Committee.

g) The Annual Audit Reports shall be sent to the state Government.

Accordingly, After careful examination of the proposal of the Commissioner and
Inspector General (R&S), A.P. Government hereby accord permission to C&IG, R&S,
AP to form a S-CARD society under Andhra Pradesh Societies Registration Act, 2001
(Act No.35 of 2001) and to deposit all the user charges collected to the account
maintained by S-CARD society and to utilize user charges funds for development
of CARD project and incidental expenditure under e-Governance projects as per the
bylaws of the society (as Annexure-l, II to this order).

(BY ORDER AND IN THE NAME OF THE GOVERNOR OF ANDHRA PRADESH)

Dr. K.S. JAWAHAR REDDY
CHIEF SECRETARY TO GOVERNMENT

satisfaction of its debts and liabilities any property whatsoever, The same shall not
be paid to or distributed among the members of the society or any of them, but shall
be dealt with in the manner provided in the Andhra Pradesh Societies Registration
Act, 2001.

Annexure-Il
RULES AND REGULATIONS (BYE-LAWS):

 

Name of the Society The name of the Society will be "Society for Computer Aided
Administration of Registration Department of Andhra Pradesh (herein after referred to as
S-CARD AP)

Registered Office: The registered office of the Society shall be at Office of the
Commissioner and Inspector General of Registration and Stamps, A.P., Vijayawada in the
State of Andhra Pradesh.

Whereas it has been considered necessary to use available technology for rendering
simple, better, transparent and user friendly services to the public in Registration offices
in Andhra Pradesh, and Whereas computerized registration procedure has been running
successfully in Registration offices, and Whereas this Society is being formed at state
level to discharge these objectives, The "Society for Computer Aided Administration of
Registration Department of Andhra Pradesh (herein after referred to as S-CARD AP)shall
be governed by the following Rules:-

1. Definitions:
In these Rules, unless the context otherwise requires: -

|. The Society" means the "Society for Computer Aided Administration of
Registration Department of Andhra Pradesh, herein after referred to as S- CARD
AP in abbreviated form, "The Committee" means the Executive Committee
(Governing Body)

Il. of the Society.
Ill "The Officers" means the member of the Executive Committee,

lV. "The Inspector General of Registration" means the Inspector General of
Registration appointed by the State Government under section 3 of the
Registration Act, 1908.

V. "The District Registrar" means the Collector who has been appointed ex- officio
District Registrar under section 6 of the Registration Act, 1908.

VI. "Rule means any of the Rules and Regulations of the Society".

Vil. "Year" means a period of twelve months beginning from 1rtApril and ending on
31st March of next calendar year.

Vill. "Act" means Andhra Pradesh Societies Registration Act, 2001.

IX. "Registration Department" means Registration Department of Government Of

Andhra Pradesh.

4. General Council:

a.

The General Council shall be the apex decision making body of the Society and
shall endeavour to achieve the objectives of the Society.

General Council of the Society shall consist of all its members.

The Secretary(Registration and Stamps), Govt. of Andhra Pradesh shall be the
Chair Person of the General Council, who will preside over the meetings of the
General council.

The General Council shall meet at least once a year Special meeting of the General
Council may also be called by the chairperson any time Incase of necessity.
The meeting of the General Council shall be convened by the chairperson of
the General Council at a time and place decided by him with the consultation of
Executive Committee.

The General Council shall have the power to amend the Memorandum, Rules
and Executive Committee if 3/5 of its members vote in favor of the amendment
which will become effective only after the approval of the State Govt. and
communication to the Society.

y2:¥ J

 
 

 

The General Council shall pass the annual budget of the Society and shall appoint
a registered chartered accountant to audit the accounts of the Society. It shall
also consider the Annual Report and audited Annual Accounts of the Society for
the preceding year along with the Audit certificate, the Audit Report and replies
of the Society to the observations contained in the Audit Report.

The quorum for General Council Meetings shall be 1/3 of its total members. No
quorum shall be required for an adjourned meeting.

Every notice calling a meeting of the General Council shall state the date, time
and place at which such meeting will be held and shall be served upon every
member of the General Council not less than ten clear days before the day
appointed for the meeting.

The accidental omission to give notice to, or the non-receipt of notice by a
member shall not invalidate the proceedings of the meeting.

Each member of the General Council shall have one vote
The presiding officer shall, in addition, have a casting vote in case of tie.

The General Council shall, perform its functions, notwithstanding any vacancy
therein and notwithstanding any defect in appointment, or nomination of any
member and no act or proceedings of the General Council shall be invalidated or
nullified merely by reason of the existence of any vacancy therein or any defect
in the appointment or nomination of any member.

Where for any reason it is not possible for the General Council to meet, any
business which it may be necessary for the General Council to perform may be
carried out by taking the approval of the Chairperson after due recommendation
by the Executive Committee, Provided that the post-facto approval of the General
Council shall be obtained in its next meeting.

The Secretary of the Executive Committee shall cause the proceedings/ minutes
of the meetings of the General Council to be maintained.

experts in its meetings or in the meetings of Sub-Committee as per necessity.

8. The Executive Committee may take the part time services of the employees
of Registration or any other Department of the State Government or of Govt
of India or Govt of Andhra Pradesh undertakings. Such employees may be
paid any additional pay or remuneration, if the Rules of the concerned Govt or
undertakings permit so. An employee may however, be compensated for any
expenditure incurred by him in connection with his services to the Society.

to sign and verify plaints, written statements, execution and other petitions,
applications, affidavits, objections, memorandum of appeal or other pleadings
to be fled before any judicial or quasi judicial authorities or arbitrators to
enforce judgment execute any decree or order, of any judicial/quasi-judicial
authorities or to satisfy the same and/or to realize or withdraw money from
any court, persons or other authorities In execution of such decree or order, to
sign vakalathnamas and to exercise all financial powers as delegated by the
Executive Committee.

h. Subject to the provisions of sub-clause (e) above, The President shall be:
competent to execute contracts, collaboration agreements). general/special
Instruments, service agreements/agreements containing arbitration clauses
Indemnity bonds and deeds in respect of/or connected with lease/ license
or other deed(s) of a legal character of whatever; description, power(s) of
attorney to enforce guarantee or any other legal rights, incur legal expenses
and to act as agent of the Society for any purpose whatsoever relating to the
affairs of the S-CARD AP.

i. To spend up to Rs. 10,00,000/- to meet the exigencies, Provided that all such
expenditures shall be put in the next meeting of the Executive Committee and
its post facto sanction shall be obtained.

  
LC

(B) Secretary:
a. To organize the meetings of Executive Committee.
b. To prepare the agenda for the meetings of Executive Committee.

c. Tonote down the proceedings of the meetings and maintain them in proceeding
register.

d. To maintain the Income and expenditure of the Society.
e. To prepare Annual Report of the Society.
f. To get the income & expenditure of the Society audited.

g. To correspond on behalf of society. But for important correspondences he
shall seek the approval of the President before issuing them.

h. To spend up to Rs. 1,00,000/- to meet the exigencies, Provided that all such
expenditures shall be put in the next meeting of the Executive Committee and
its post facto sanction shall be obtained.

7. Funds of the Society
(1) The funds of the Society shall consist of the following:

a. Funds collected by way of user charges from registering public for rendering
computerized services through the department.

b. Fees and charges imposed by the Society for consultancy services rendered
by it in other state;

(2) Management of Funds:
a. The bankers of S-CARD AP shall be any of the Public Sector bank only

b. A Personal Deposit account shall be opened in the joint names of President
and Secretary of the Executive Committee of the Society.

c. All the withdrawals from bank shall be made by cheque under the joint
signature of President and Secretary.

8. Role of NIC: NIC shall be the technical advisor of the society and shall not be involved
In financial decisions of the society.

9. Powers of State Government :-

The State Government shall have the powers to give directions, as are not Inconsistent
with the Memorandum of Association and Rules of S-CARD AP regarding policy matters
from time to time which shall be binding on the S-CARD AP. Such directions shall be in
writing,

 
 

& ° .».”»”»+x,xLEZ 7

10. Accounts and Audit and Annual Report:

 

1. The Society shall cause accounts to be kept of all its money and properties in
respect of its affairs. The accounts shall be maintained as per existing Rules and
Instructions as amended from time to time and practices of the Government of
Andhra Pradesh.

2. Aregistered Chartered Accountant appointed by the General Council shall audit
annual accounts of S-CARD AP every year. The Govt. of Andhra Pradesh or
Inspector General of Registration to get its account audited by any Chartered
Accountant.

3. The report of Audit shall be placed before the President and shall also be sent to
the state government.

4. The Annual Report of the activities of S-CARD AP including all works undertaken
and various achievements made, during a year together with thel Audited Annual
Accounts (Viz., Receipts and Payments Accounts, Income and Expenditure
Accounts and Balance Sheet) for the year, the Audit Certificate, the Audit Report
and replies of S-CARD AP to the observations contained in the Audit Report shall
be placed before the General Council at its next annual general meeting, through
the Executive Committee.

5. The General Council shall cause a copy of the Audit Report along with its
observations thereon to be submitted to the Government of Andhra Pradesh
after the annual general meeting.

11. Office of S-CARD AP-

The Principal office of S-CARD AP shall be co-located with office of Commissioner &
Inspector General in the State of Andhra Pradesh.

12. Service of Notice-

1. A notice may be served upon any member of S-CARD AP either personally by
obtaining signature in notice book or by registered post at the address mentioned
in the roll of members.

2. Any notice so served by registered post shall be deemed to be served on the day
following that on which it is posted.

13. Alteration or Extension of the Objectives of the Society -

Subject to the relevant provisions of the Andhra Pradesh Societies Registration Act,
2001, the General Council may, with the prior approval of the Government of Andhra
Pradesh, alter, extend or abridge any objective or objectives for which the Society is
established.

 
Se)

14. Amendment of the Rules -

With the prior concurrence of the Government of Andhra Pradesh, Rules of the
Society may be altered at any time by a resolution when voted by three fifth of the
members present at a meeting of the General CounciL convened for this purpose in
accordance with the rules of the Society subject to provisions of Andhra Pradesh
Societies Registration Act, 2001.

15. Dissolution of the Society:

Subject to the provision of the Andhra Pradesh Societies Registration Act, 2001,
the decision to dissolve the Society, can be taken by the General Council with prior
approval of the Government of Andhra Pradesh.

Dr. K.S. JAWAHAR REDDY
CHIEF SECRETARY TO GOVERNMENT
